# BLACKBOARD.md — Price-Space LLM

*Single writer per section. Discipline, not code-enforced. See `sdd-kit-2/AGENTS.md` § The BLACKBOARD protocol.*

---

## Surfaced for review

*Agent + Architect. Halts, partials, comprehension affirmations, Vocabulary-Session preflight, Rubber Duck observations marked `surfaced`.*

- **2026-09-27 (Agent, Sprints 118-119 closed; Architect direction for next phase)** — An outside audit of the public repo claimed the linear baseline was undertrained. Sprint 118 tested it: false (linear peaks at step 800 at lr 1e-3, overfits after), but tuned target-history baselines (linear 3.256, GRU 3.219, MLP 3.212, 5 seeds each) cut the transformer's reported 3.29% margin to 1.0-2.3%. Those baselines are not the product spec's pre-registered ones (multi-channel linear and MLP, ~1M-param GRU/TCN, target-only ablation on normalized data); none of those was built, so no pre-registered log-loss gate was measured. Sprint 119 closed a held-out leak: `train.py` never called the guard, `tokens.latest.pt` resolved to held-out tokens, and two train smokes had been fitting on it. A same-day self-audit found two wrong claims in the first draft of the public correction (gates "fail"; channels "worth 0.03 nats"); both rewritten before commit. **Architect direction (chat, 2026-09-27):** 15-minute Alpha-Vantage bars were the toy version; the next phase is acquiring all of the data. Sprint 120 (successor spec Q1) drafts the acquisition plan: every instrument and resolution each vendor carries, full history, prices read from vendor pages at drafting time. Resolved the same day under the new decision-routing rule (`WORKING_AGREEMENT.md`): successor held-out window fixed at 2025-07-01 through 2026-06-30, three looks, before any pull (most recent year, never read; the 2024-01..2025-06 window is spent and joins training); the spec's sequencing paragraph now puts acquisition first, with Questions 2 and 3 on the acquired corpus; local `data/tokenized/tokens.latest.pt` repointed from the held-out tokens to the normalized 2015-2022 training artifact.

- **2026-08-26 (Agent, Sprint 117 close + successor spec landed + spec iteration on outside feedback)** — Initial phase (Phases A through H) closed. Sprint 117 fixed a scale bug in the simulator (`derive_prediction_scalars` produced a raw log-return under a name that promised a vol-normalized one; on SPY 15-min bars the two scales differed by ~600x), ran the held-out simulator against the Sprint 115 mixer checkpoint, and recorded the result: Sharpe minus SE fails at every threshold; the log-loss edge does not translate to a directional trading edge. Full breakdown at `reports/phase-h-close.md`; post-mortem at `postmortems/simulator-scale.md`. Look log now shows 2 of 3 spent (one look remaining). Repository renamed to `large-price-model` on GitHub; Python package name `price_space_llm` unchanged for import stability + AWS tag stability. Docs reorganized: `postmortems/` and `reports/` split out of `reviews/`. Standard project files added (README, LICENSE Apache 2.0, SECURITY, CHANGELOG). The **successor spec landed at `specs/large-price-model-v1.md`** and iterated through two rounds of outside feedback. It names three co-equal open research questions and describes what a first pass at each looks like: **(1) how do we get the data we need, and what data is that** — a research task about acquisition; no database is pre-imagined; the resolution/instrument-set/vendor/storage matrix has to be worked out; the section ends with an "on sequencing" paragraph naming the alternative diagnose-first frame and stating why this spec takes data-first while running Q2 diagnostics in parallel. **(2) what does the inside of the model need to look like, and how do we get it there** — grounded in Phase H's shape-vs-mean finding; three settled-technique probes (layer probes, regime axes, context clusters) plus two retraining experiments (variance retargeting against a GARCH baseline; synthetic controls that separate architectural fault from target unpredictability); newer end (sparse autoencoders, activation steering) named as research on top. **(3) which metaphors from other domains transfer to price data** — the North Star; market-as-conversation framing (LLM simulates a language user; a price model similarly simulates a market participant); seven candidate transfers now on file (masked pretraining, contrastive, retrieval, patching, MoE, chain-of-thought, and a newly added continuous parametric output with CRPS scoring). The opening of the spec was rewritten to include the plain-English decomposition of returns into conditional mean + conditional variance (model captured variance not mean) and the arbitrage-saturation explanation for the null on mean at SPY 15-min. A companion spec `specs/text-integration-v1.md` lands as further-out horizon work: arbitrary-text integration (books, social media, science, government, cultural artifacts) via universal embeddings + FAISS retrieval + cross-attention fusion + latent-regime compression, tested via CRPS on distributional prediction. A third round of outside feedback then landed as a second companion spec `specs/text-market-experiments-v1.md` — eight self-contained research programs: (1) information diffusion latency as target, (2) counterfactual price formation via conditional generative models, (3) semantic state inversion (price-to-narrative generation), (4) topological regime discovery via textual embeddings with persistent homology, (5) information entropy divergence as a volatility leading indicator, (6) learnable temporal granularity via multi-scale pyramid networks, (7) systematic ablation as a discovery engine, (8) self-supervised alignment (CLIP-style text-price contrastive foundation). Programs 6, 7, and 8 also fold as small edits into the main spec (multi-scale as a Q3 candidate transfer; systematic ablation as a new Q2 retraining experiment; contrastive bullet in Q3 points at Program 8 for the full version). Program 8 is called out in `text-integration-v1.md` as the strongest candidate pretraining objective for the text-price architecture. Main spec references both companions in a "Companion specs" section right before the first-sprint pointer. Constraints carried: no hosted trackers; SDD discipline; held-out data sacred (successor declares its own window + budget). Sprint 118 opens by drafting the comparison matrix for Question 1. **Architect ratification requested on the successor spec + companion** — either accept as v1 or return with edits; downstream sprints unblock on that Decision.

- **2026-08-17 (Agent, drift-watchlist retirement: last-live item awaits Decision)** — Probe module's `EXPECTED_BARS_PER_TRADING_MONTH = 21 * 26 = 546` assumes regular-trading-hours only. Alpha-Vantage's `TIME_SERIES_INTRADAY` defaults to `extended_hours=true`, returning ~1200-1600 bars per month for a liquid symbol. Current probe path clamps over-count to `missing_fraction=0` — harmless for the accept/drop verdict today (every current channel passes at the extended-hours count). Live risk: any future ingest that explicitly toggles `extended_hours=false` would probe against a wrong denominator and drop legitimate channels at ~0.08 reported missing. **Architect chooses one of three paths and files to `## Decisions`:** (a) **Adopt `pandas_market_calendars` new dep** — compute real trading-day counts per exchange calendar, both for the probe and downstream schedule-aware code (holidays, half-days, Juneteenth). Real cost: one small dep + calendar-based denominator computation in probe.py; downstream candidates (Sprint 048's FOMC/CPI event tables, Sprint 065's heldout guard) get a shared calendar. (b) **Add `extended_hours` toggle** — probe carries an `extended_hours: bool` param; denominator adapts. Fixes the future-toggle risk without a new dep. Small. (c) **Ratify the current shape** — extended-hours-default is the AV default; `missing_fraction=0` clamp is safe; the "future toggle" path is speculative. Note in `WORKING_AGREEMENT § Ingest` and move on. Cheapest. Revisit trigger: Architect Decision closes this Surfaced entry AND the reclassified drift entry below.

Fix path: redefine to target-non-null semantics (target row valid ⇔ target feature block non-null and target itself not `-100`) OR drop the field. Own sprint per hard rule 6 — one file (`tokenizer/bucketize.py`) + one consumer (`model/dataset.py`) + test update + re-materialize the two `.pt` artifacts. Revisit trigger: any sprint that adds a live consumer of `mask` (Sprint 088 held-out evaluation likely) — that sprint gets an all-False field and produces wrong holdout coverage.

- **2026-08-13 (Agent, from reviews/pre-gpu-deploy-holistic-review.md §2.2)** — Frozen normalizer prescribed by tech-arch §6 does not exist AND its drift diagnostic prescribed by product-spec line 251 has no signal home. Tech-arch requires `expanding-window causal z-score computed on the training partition only, then frozen`, persisted to `artifacts/{run_id}/normalizers.pt`. Actual code (`features/compute.py`) computes a per-bar `rolling_z_score_20` using `polars.rolling_mean(window_size=20)` — a trailing/backward window that IS causal per-bar (no future leak) but is a rolling scaler, not a training-partition-frozen scaler. Zero writes to `normalizers.pt`. Zero emit sites for `NORMALIZER_FITTED` and `NORMALIZER_STATE_WRITTEN`. Product-spec line 251 names "Frozen normalizer going stale" as a specific v1 risk with a required diagnostic: "plot the normalized-feature distribution on the holdout against training. Track it as a failure mode." That diagnostic has no signal home in the current vocabulary and no plotting code. Fix path is two parts: (a) implement expanding-window training-partition-only fit + frozen persistence to `normalizers.pt`, wire `NORMALIZER_FITTED` + `NORMALIZER_STATE_WRITTEN`; (b) add a `NORMALIZER_DRIFT_MEASURED` tag (v0.4 candidate) with payload `feature_name`, `train_dist_summary`, `holdout_dist_summary`, `ks_statistic`, `p_value`, and wire the evaluator to emit it per feature on holdout. Alternative: ratify the deviation in a rationale-doc entry naming rolling-window as the v1 substitute and drift-diagnostic as v2. Silence is not an option — the spec makes two specific promises the code keeps neither of. Revisit trigger: the sprint that lands the first non-smoke training run.

- **2026-08-13 (Agent, from reviews/pre-gpu-deploy-holistic-review.md §2.4)** — Fixed EST offset (`US_EASTERN_OFFSET = timedelta(hours=-5)` in `src/price_space_llm/ingestion/alphavantage.py:33`) is DST-wrong for ~7 months per year. Uses: alphavantage extractors (intraday :63, options :232, BBO :261, index :317) and alignment (`join.py:29, 148`). Verified impact on current pipeline: SPY intraday bars and RTH grid both stamp through the same fixed offset, so alignment is internally consistent (no relative drift within the SPY channel — the review's "correctness bug for any training" is overstated here). VIX INDEX_DATA's `known_at` IS drift-affected during EDT months: today's VIX close labelled 21:01 UTC when real EDT close is 20:00 UTC — model sees prior day's VIX close for ~1 extra bar during ~7 months/year. The moment external time sources join (macro releases in real US Eastern, BLS timestamps, etc.), everything shifts. Fix: adopt `zoneinfo("America/New_York")` (stdlib, 3.9+), replace `US_EASTERN_OFFSET` and `timezone(US_EASTERN_OFFSET)` call sites, add a test that a March bar and a November bar both round-trip correctly. Requires re-running alignment on the full cached corpus (114 SPY monthly caches + 1 VIX INDEX_DATA already on disk; ingest is cache-hit; align is 1.75s). Revisit trigger: before the first macro channel lands OR before the first held-out evaluation whose Sharpe claim rests on time-accurate alignment.

- **2026-08-13 (Agent, from parallel spec re-read audit during Sprint 041)** — Nineteen additional spec-vs-code gaps NOT covered by the pre-GPU review's 12 items. Ranked by severity below with file/line evidence.

  **SEVERE (1):** Transformer input is discrete bucket-IDs, not continuous multi-channel state. `src/price_space_llm/model/transformer.py:36` uses `nn.Embedding(vocab_size, d_model)`; forward takes `tokens: Tensor(B,T)` of bucket ids. Spec §7.2 requires `MarketStateEmbedder` — per-channel `nn.Linear(F_c, d_model)` summed. Distinct from the "3 vs 19 channels" surfacing: even with 19 channels wired, this model would consume none of them. The current transformer is fundamentally the target-only-on-bucket-ids sequence model — a lower ceiling than the spec's multi-channel state model. Val_top-1 ≈ 0.10 in the Sprint 040 smoke reflects this: the model has no market_context input. GPU spend on the current architecture produces numbers for a model the spec never defined.

  **HIGH (8):**
  - Aligned parquet omits three spec-mandated "load-bearing" columns per channel: `missing_mask__*`, `age_since_known_at__*`, `observed_at_this_grid_step__*`. `alignment/join.py:196-206` writes only `{key}__close` + `{key}__known_at`. Spec §5 § "The staleness pair" explicit on this.
  - Aligned parquet carries close only; ingestion parses OHLCV then discards. `alignment/join.py:79-86` reads `bar["4. close"]` only. Downstream `bar_shape`, `range_pct`, `volume_z_100`, `spread_proxy`, `dollar_volume` (spec §6) are impossible to compute.
  - `is_overnight_gap` flag not computed. Product-spec §Channels + tech-arch §6 mandate it with sample code. Zero references in `features/compute.py`.
  - `bucket_stats.json` schema is wrong. `tokenizer/bucketize.py:24-32,136-159` writes only `{n_buckets, target_symbol, dates, row_count, edges}`. Spec §7.1 requires per-bucket `bucket_lower`, `bucket_upper`, `bucket_train_mean`, `bucket_train_median`, `bucket_train_frequency`. Simulator's decode of outer buckets to expected return requires `bucket_train_mean`; `baselines.py:189-213` currently fakes it from edge midpoints.
  - `TargetOnlyBaseline` is a marginal-frequency predictor, not the spec's ablation. `src/price_space_llm/baselines.py:77-89` broadcasts training marginal P(bucket). Spec §Baselines: "same architecture as the default transformer, every non-target channel zeroed at the embedder — the whole thesis rides on this comparison." The gate is currently meaningless.
  - `REQUIRED_DELTA_PCT` uses `magnitude_weighted` instead of `gru_tcn`. `baselines.py:34-38`. Spec §Success gates: linear 10%, target_only 5%, gru_tcn 5%. Magnitude-weighted is an ablation, not a gate.
  - Optimizer is Adam; no AdamW, no cosine schedule, no 2000-step warmup, no bf16. `model/trainer.py:151`. Spec §9.2 explicit on all four.
  - No simulator subsystem at all. No `simulation/` dir, no `SimResult`, no bar walker, no `scripts/simulate.py`, no capacity sweep, no block-bootstrap Sharpe. Spec §11 + §11.5 + Success gates require them. Broader than the "unemitted tags" surfacing.
  - No `experiments/logbook.csv` writer. Product-spec + tech-arch §13 require `scripts/register_run.py` appending one row per training run. Script absent; `experiments/` dir does not exist.

  **MEDIUM (5):**
  - Session flags (`minute_of_day`, `day_of_week`, sin/cos) not computed. Spec §Channels "at every 15-minute timestep." Zero references.
  - Regime evaluator ignores real VIX. `evaluation/regimes.py:5-10` + `evaluation/evaluate.py:109` still bucket regimes from `target__{sym}__rolling_std_20`, though Sprint 038 landed VIX in the aligned frame.
  - Pre-commit `check_test_look.sh` filesystem-date guard missing. Tech-arch §13 mandates it in addition to the runtime log. `testlook.py` only manages the JSONL budget.
  - Checkpoint selection is "latest," not "top-K by val NLL." `trainer.py:250-255` symlinks `latest.pt` to newest step. Spec §9.3: "Keep top-K by validation NLL."
  - Purged `H`-bar embargo at split boundaries not applied. `evaluate.py:184-187` and `model/dataset.py:split_tokens` slice contiguously. Spec §Testing + §9.2 require the gap.

  **LOW (4):**
  - Tokenized artifact lacks the `meta` dict (`run_id, config hash, git sha, data hash, target_symbol, channel_coverage_sha`). Tech-arch §5.
  - Channel-projection cosine diagnostic (tech-arch §10) not computed anywhere. Declared exploratory.
  - Bucket-frequency drift diagnostic covers train-vs-val only. Spec §10 also requires train-vs-test at final evaluation.
  - `SENSITIVITY_PLOT_GENERATED` (Sharpe-vs-kappa 0.5x-2x) — the plot artifact itself missing, not just the tag. Spec §11.2.

  **Aggregate reading.** Between the pre-GPU review's 12 items and this audit's 19, at least three specification claims are structurally unmeasurable in the current code: the target-only baseline gate (bad baseline shape), the gru_tcn baseline gate (baseline missing entirely), and the multi-channel-lift claim (transformer consumes bucket-ids only). None are fixable by tuning; each needs a code shift. Revisit trigger: **before any GPU-driven training spend that will claim to test any of the pre-registered gates**. The current transformer is a defensible sub-model to smoke, not a defensible model to launch v1 on.

- **2026-08-12 (Agent, from reviews/sprints-030-033-ml-pipeline-review.md §2)** — RoPE deviation. Tech-arch §8 and §Model glossary name rotary positional embeddings. `src/price_space_llm/model/transformer.py:37` declares `nn.Embedding(config.context_len, config.d_model)` for positions and `:64` adds them to token embeddings — the learned-absolute pattern. WORKING_AGREEMENT:50 still lists the model as "Causal decoder, RoPE, pre-LN, GELU, causal SDPA." Sprint 031 shipped the substitution without naming it on the card. Two paths: (a) land RoPE — PyTorch has no built-in, the reference implementation is ~30 lines applied inside attention `q, k`, requires ditching `nn.TransformerEncoder` for a hand-written block; (b) ratify learned-absolute as v1, RoPE as v2 upgrade — defensible on a 519-token corpus where the position table cost is trivial. Architect chooses. Revisit trigger: the sprint that runs the size sweep (position-table cost scales with d_model × context_len; RoPE is free).

- **2026-08-12 (Agent, from reviews/sprints-030-033-ml-pipeline-review.md §4)** — `WINDOW_SAMPLED.start_position` semantics. `src/price_space_llm/model/trainer.py:158` emits `start_position=int(step)` with the comment "deterministic proxy; sampler returns starts per-batch." Payload purports to name the window's start position in the token stream but carries the training step number. A trace reader sees `start_position=25` and cannot tell whether it is token 25 or step 25. Smaller version of the Sprint 020 `_error_shim` pattern. Two fixes: (i) route `WindowSampler.sample()`'s real starts out and emit the first-batch start; use 0 for later windows, or (ii) rename the payload field to match the actual semantics. `WINDOW_SAMPLED` fires per training step rather than per window inside a batch, so consider whether the tag itself should fire batch_size × more often. Halt-or-articulate applies at next trainer touch.

- **2026-08-12 Claude Code (Opus 4.7)** — HALT (RETRACTED — see entry above): `bridge_mapping_required` (Sprint 034 = cost calibration, deferred). Tech-arch §11.1's cost model has two components: a `SpreadScaler` fit against observed BBO half-spread over the training window, and a `Kappa` impact coefficient fit against realized market-impact data. Alpha-Vantage (my sole data source) publishes 15-min OHLCV bars — no BBO snapshots, no per-trade impact. Substituting bar-range (high - low) as a spread proxy is a defensible half-measure for the spread scaler; fitting Kappa without impact data means fabricating values, which is the exact Sprint 020 anti-pattern (widen the shim to make the emitter stop refusing). Sprint 034 = cost calibration is BLOCKED on either (a) a real BBO source (Databento, IEX Cloud DEEP feed, or an academic archive), or (b) a real impact data source (Kyle's-lambda proxy from tick data, or a canonical constant from published Almgren-Chriss with `kappa_se=0` and an honest "no fit; canonical value" note in the sprint card — still weakens the signal). Filed as adjacent to the 2026-08-10 BBO drift-watchlist entry. Sprint 034 pivots to `testlook` category instead — two tags, self-contained, no data dependencies.

- **2026-08-12 Claude Code (Opus 4.7)** — SURFACED (Architect audit): pattern of documented-SDK adoption without a filed `bridge_mapping_required` halt. Since Sprint 018's Alpha-Vantage MCP halt, six PyPI packages have landed on the deps line without a matching halt entry: `httpx` (Sprint 019), `polars` + `pyarrow` (Sprint 025), `pydantic` (Sprint 028), `torch` + `numpy` (Sprint 031), `hypothesis` (Sprint 029 dev). All adopted from published docs; none surprised at first use. Alpha-Vantage MCP surprised because its actual tool signatures diverged from `WORKING_AGREEMENT`'s stub — the halt was information-gathering, not ceremony. Two defensible readings of the rule: **(a) literal** — any first-use of an external SDK requires a `bridge_mapping_required` halt; six retroactive halts owed. **(b) intent-based** — the halt fires when the SDK's actual surface is expected to diverge from priors (undocumented tools, agent-only APIs, network protocols with vendor-specific idioms); standard PyPI packages with published docs and stable APIs adopt on the sprint card's dep line, articulated in the sprint notes if any behavior surprises land during use. **Recommendation: (b), codified.** `WORKING_AGREEMENT § External SDK bridge mappings` gains a rule: bridge_mapping_required halts on (i) network APIs whose actual response shape is not pinned in a public spec, (ii) agent-only tool namespaces, (iii) SDKs where the assumed surface has diverged from the documented one at any prior sprint. Standard PyPI adoptions (httpx, polars, pyarrow, pydantic, torch, numpy, hypothesis) are non-halt events; their dep pin + version + one-line note in the sprint card's `notes` is the record. Architect ratifies (a), (b), or a variant.

- **2026-08-11 Claude Code (Opus 4.7)** — HALT: `vocabulary_change_required` (Sprint 023). v0.2's `CHANNEL_REJECTED` and `CHANNEL_COVERAGE_ASSESSED` both require `earliest_timestamp: datetime_utc`. When zero sample-date fetches succeed for a channel, there is no earliest_timestamp — no observation was made. The vocabulary offers no honest way to serialize the survey outcome. Three options, one recommendation:
  - **(a)** Mark `earliest_timestamp` and `latest_timestamp` optional on both tags. Smallest change; a Reviewer reading `earliest_timestamp: null` learns "no observation was made" from context.
  - **(b)** Add a new tag `CHANNEL_FETCH_FAILED` (ingest/incident) whose payload names exception class + vendor error string + channel/symbol/source. Cleaner separation of "no data returned" from "vendor call itself broke"; requires an incident-stratum tag under `probe` category. Existing `CHANNEL_REJECTED` handles "we got some data but not enough"; new tag handles "we got no response at all."
  - **(c)** Both — (a) covers legitimate "empty month for a valid symbol" (rare); (b) covers exception-driven failures.
  - **Recommendation: (b).** Exception-driven failures and low-observation channels are semantically different events. Reader shouldn't need context to tell them apart. Cost: one new tag, one Layer-4 pairing constraint (`CHANNEL_FETCH_FAILED → CHANNEL_COVERAGE_ASSESSED(verdict=dropped)`), one Layer-7 diagnostic constraint (`error_message` non-empty). No breaking change to existing tags.
  - Resume: Architect writes to `## Decisions` picking (a), (b), or (c). Sprint 023 then rips out the two lie paths (blocking on the choice for which reason enum to route to). If the halt takes more than one turn, Sprint 020's live smoke stays broken — probe exits nonzero on the first fetcher exception, honestly.

- **2026-08-09 Claude Code (Opus 4.7)** — COMPREHENSION_AFFIRMATION. I have read, in full, `sdd-kit-2/README.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `TECHNIQUES.md`, `ADDENDUMS.md`, `process-not-prompt-research.md`, `process-not-prompt-summary.md`, `foundations/01-04`, `grammar/README.md` and `PRINCIPLES.md` and `BOOTSTRAP.md`, every file under `templates/`, `lib/sdd.py`, and the whole `example/wordcount/` project including its three sprint cards and BLACKBOARD. I have also read the project's inputs in full: `specs/product-spec-v4.md`, `specs/technical-architecture-v4.md`, `reviews/review.md`, `research/proposal.md`, `research/price-space-llm-analysis.md`, `research/build-guide.md`. This project is **Price-Space LLM v1** — a causal decoder transformer over 15-minute multi-channel market state on SPY, predicting a 32-bucket vol-normalized forward return distribution, run through a cost-aware simulator on a 2024-01-01 through 2025-06-30 held-out window; success is defined by pre-registered gates in the product spec (validation NLL beats named baselines, ECE below 0.05, held-out Sharpe with attached SE and block-bootstrap distribution, capacity above $1M, no data leakage). SDD at root replaces the lossy human-description step with typed signals the running program emits; the vocabulary is the contract, signals are not logs, and the description step is the compression format the kit deletes. The kit's canonical loop here is: I read the next sprint card from `sprints/`, execute via `Read`/`Write`/`Edit`, return a Signal Report per `templates/SIGNAL_REPORT.md`, run the Rubber Duck Pass at sprint close in three steps (narration → six-category observations → four-state dispositions), write to BLACKBOARD sections per single-writer discipline, and never write to `## Decisions` — Architect-only. For this project specifically: the class is Data science / ML training plus CLI (per TECHNIQUES.md §2), so `SESSION_INIT` carries dataset + model + vocab versions; training loops emit per-step / per-epoch / per-run signals at three strata; metric snapshots are artifacts; every sprint declares its determinism budget as bit-deterministic or statistically deterministic. Bridge mappings for PyTorch, the Alpha-Vantage MCP, Hydra, Pydantic v2, Polars, and W&B are stubs in `WORKING_AGREEMENT.md` — the first sprint that imports any of them halts with `bridge_mapping_required` if the actual surface is not on file, which is the rule that stops soundfield-round-13-style guess-and-iterate. The four grading traps from Addendum D bind hard here: signals drive but cannot grade (the engine that produced the bytes cannot judge them); a test that has retyped the code it is testing grades a copy of the code; a gate nobody has watched fail is not a gate; a correctness suite cannot see a performance failure. Concretely: the calibrated cost model, an independent-reader check on any written artifact (`bucket_stats.json` and `normalizers.pt` at minimum), and the target-only ablation against the full transformer are the outside gates for this project. The hard rule binding me: halt-and-articulate. When the sprint's scope is unclear, when the vocabulary lacks a needed tag, when a bridge mapping is missing, when the observation contract is absent from a behaviour-touching sprint, I write a typed halt entry here and stop.

## Decisions

*Architect-only, append-only.*

- **2026-08-17 (Architect)** — Historical SPY BBO for full-window cost calibration ratified as **path (b) Corwin-Schultz OHL proxy + 2024 bias correction**; path (a) Databento revisit reserved after the first couple of real GPU runs (Sprint 084+ sweep). Rationale: land the cheapest defensible calibration now, measure how much it matters against the pre-registered gates once real Sharpe numbers exist, then decide whether $100-500 for ground-truth BBO buys meaningful additional resolution. Sprint 091 opens as: extend `src/price_space_llm/cost_calibration/spread.py` with a Corwin-Schultz estimator on the aligned OHLC series; fit CS on the 2024 window where Sprint 035 has ground-truth BBO; compute `bias_ratio = mean(Sprint035_2024_known_BBO) / mean(CS_2024_estimate)`; apply the scalar bias correction to the CS estimates on the 2015-2022 training window; refit the frozen `SpreadScaler` against the bias-corrected series; keep Sprint 035's `Kappa` unchanged (Kyle-lambda signal near-zero at 15-min bars regardless of vendor). The v1 report's cost-model section must name the residual regime-dependent bias explicitly. Revisit trigger: after Sprint 084 model-size sweep + Sprint 088 first held-out evaluation — if the Sharpe-gate margin is inside 20% of the "Sharpe − SE > 0" threshold, path (a) buys the resolution to defend the claim; if the margin is comfortable, path (b) suffices. Phase F opens on Sprint 091.

- **2026-08-16 (Architect)** — RoPE ratified for the model; implementation deferred past the pre-GPU work. The learned-absolute `nn.Embedding(context_len, d_model)` at `model/transformer.py:36` stands as v1 for now. RoPE lands as a separate sprint after the Phase E ablation configs (Sprints 073-078) close. Phase F cost calibration + simulator (roadmap 073-078, real Sprints 079 onward) is unblocked: no historical-BBO Decision is required to open it. Sprint 073 = model-size configs (roadmap 067) opens next.

- **2026-08-14 (Architect)** — UUP ratified as DXY proxy for v1. Alpha-Vantage carries no DXY (ICE Data Services index; `INDEX_CATALOG` lists only CBOE/S&P indices; `FX_INTRADAY` reaches ~2 months back only, no historical intraday depth). Databento's Plus tier ($1,750/mo annual) is the only vendor path with 15+ years of intraday FX; not pursued. UUP tracks the DB Long USD Currency Portfolio Index (same six currencies as ICE DXY, roughly matched weights); daily-return correlation ~0.98-0.99, intraday correlation ~0.95+ in liquid hours. Expense ratio 0.77%/yr, quarterly futures-roll basis events, and ETF tracking error < 5bp all live in cumulative levels rather than per-bar log returns; the model consumes log returns and a rolling volume z-score per spec §Channels, where these effects are small. UUP delivers real intraday variation; the daily-forward-filled alternative (FX_DAILY basket) would collapse to a step function and trigger `FEATURE_COMPUTATION_FAILED` on rolling-window features. Manifest `market_context__UUP` entry documents the substitution. Sprint 044 already landed UUP in the pull; this Decision ratifies. Full-window BBO cost calibration (Sprint 073) also drops any Databento assumption — the fallback is a Corwin-Schultz proxy fit on OHL, or extrapolating Sprint 035's 3-month calibration to the training window; the choice lands when Sprint 073 opens.

- **2026-08-13** — Hosted trackers retracted from the project. Weights & Biases and every comparable third-party experiment-tracking service (Neptune, Comet, ClearML, DataDog for ML, etc.) is prohibited for the life of the project. Rationale: professional operations do not use them; they read as advertising; the SDD JSONL trace already captures every scalar they would upload. Sprint 040 code and docs deleted; v0.4 vocab bump removes `WANDB_UPLOAD_FAILED`; `scripts/plot_run.py` (to land in a later sprint) plots the trace to `artifacts/{run_id}/` as PNG. Extends to any future SaaS "convenience" tool: local files, self-hosted tools, and user-owned infrastructure only. Codified in `WORKING_AGREEMENT § Run tracking`.

- **2026-08-13** — Post-data-pull sprint sequence corrected. Prior 2026-08-12 Decision said "Sprint 038 = operational pull + full pipeline." Wrong scale by 20×. Training runs on rented GPU (AWS), not this 28GB laptop. Every sweep point in the model-size sweep (§8.1), context-length sweep (§9.1), four architecture ablations (§10), and baseline ladder is its own training run. Corrected sequence supersedes: **Sprint 039** = operational pull + local chain to tokens (landed); **Sprint 040** = retracted 2026-08-13 (hosted-tracker integration; see the 2026-08-13 retraction Decision above); **Sprint 041** = pre-GPU review-driven fixes (landed); **Sprints 042-090** = plans/v1-roadmap.md, phased data → features → model → baselines → sweeps → simulator → held-out.

- **2026-08-13** — VIX carried via INDEX_DATA, not TIME_SERIES_INTRADAY. Alpha-Vantage's INDEX_DATA endpoint returns VIX daily OHLC back to 1990-01-02 (9,246 rows), current-day settled by ~21:01 UTC. `TIME_SERIES_INTRADAY` refuses VIX and `^VIX` because VIX is an index, not an equity. Sprint 023 recorded the intraday rejection correctly but the framing widened over subsequent sprints into "AV has no VIX." Correction: VIX is available at daily granularity; use INDEX_DATA. The evaluator's regime code still forward-fills a real VIX signal onto the 15-min RTH grid via `join_asof(strategy="backward")` on `known_at = date + 21:01 UTC`. The prior `target__SPY__rolling_std_20` substitution can either stay as a secondary regime input or be retired once operational training runs on the real VIX; leaving that choice to the sprint that runs the size sweep. Sprint 038 lands the code, Sprint 039 lands the operational pull, Sprint 040 runs the full pipeline, Sprint 041 = simulator (renumbered from prior 039).

- **2026-08-09** — Project scope: Price-Space LLM v1 is a causal decoder transformer over 15-minute multi-channel market state on SPY, predicting a 32-bucket vol-normalized forward return distribution, run through a cost-aware simulator on a 2024-01-01 through 2025-06-30 held-out window. Success is defined by the pre-registered gates in `specs/product-spec-v4.md` §"Success gates for v1". Stack: Python 3.11, PyTorch 2.4+, single GPU (A10 or A100), ~$500 compute envelope, ~9 weeks of solo work. Purpose: test whether the specific combination of multi-channel tokenization, vol-normalized categorical target, and cost-aware decision layer produces information that survives realistic costs on chronological holdout — no cited prior system combines all three.

- **2026-08-09** — Adopted kit: `sdd-kit-2/` at project root (read-only). BLACKBOARD, KIT_DIARY, WORKING_AGREEMENT instantiated. Vocabulary Session is Sprint 0 per hard rule 12; no implementation sprint dispatches until `signals/0.1.json` and its rationale doc are locked and signed.

- **2026-08-12** — Storage-integrity path ratified (choice b of the Sprint 036 direction). Three sprints, in order: **Sprint 036 = storage integrity** (per-cache-file `.meta.json` provenance sidecar; append-only `data/raw/cache_index.jsonl`; freshness windows per tool; `artifacts/` versioned writes by `run_id` with a `latest` symlink; downstream consumers name the specific artifact `run_id` they read via `config_hash`). **Sprint 037 = multi-month CLIs** (extend `align.py`, `features.py`, `bucketize.py` with `--start-month --end-month` producing one combined file per stage per range). **Sprint 038 = operational pull + full pipeline run** (SPY + accepted market_context channels across the tech-arch training range 2022+ and held-out test range 2024-2025, then align → features → tokenize → train → calibrate → baselines → evaluate on the full window). Simulator lands after all three as Sprint 039. Cost neutrality applies throughout — endpoint access is not a constraint. Digression flagged and corrected: the earlier "run the simulator on 520 June bars" would have produced statistically meaningless numbers and should have been the multi-month pull first. Multi-month pull is not a "one-off grab-data thing" — the storage design lands first.

- **2026-08-12** — Bridge-mapping discipline codified. Path (b) ratified per the 2026-08-12 Surfaced entry. `bridge_mapping_required` halts fire when the SDK's actual surface is expected to diverge from priors (network APIs whose response shape is not pinned in a public spec, agent-only tool namespaces, or SDKs previously observed to diverge). Standard PyPI packages with published stable APIs adopt via a dep-pin plus a one-line note in the sprint card's `notes` section; no halt required. WORKING_AGREEMENT updated in the same commit. Retroactive halts NOT owed for httpx, polars, pyarrow, pydantic, torch, numpy, hypothesis — each shipped with a sprint-card note documenting the adoption.

- **2026-08-11** — Vocabulary v0.3 locked. One new tag `CHANNEL_FETCH_FAILED` (probe/incident) with six required payload fields; one new `diagnostic_required` evidence constraint on `error_message`. Loader default retargeted from `0.2.json` to `0.3.json`. v0.2 remains on disk. Ratified path (b) over paths (a) optional timestamps and (c) both. Full delta in `signals/0.3-rationale.md`.

- **2026-08-11** — Sprint 018 halt resolved. Transport: path (a) — HTTP + API key. `httpx` becomes the Alpha-Vantage transport; `ALPHAVANTAGE_API_KEY` env var carries the credential. Sprint 019 authors the fetcher module and adds the dep pin per the dep-pin rule. Sprint 020 wires the fetcher into `scripts/probe_channels.py` and runs a live smoke. Vocabulary `source: "mcp_av"` label stays — provenance tag for Alpha-Vantage data, transport-agnostic. Key material written to `.env` at project root (gitignored) and `.env.example` shipped with a placeholder.

- **2026-08-10** — Vocabulary v0.2 locked. Correction to `CAPACITY_SWEEP_COMPLETED.points` type declaration; `struct` composite kind added to Layer-2 vocabulary. Full delta in `signals/0.2-rationale.md`. Loader default retargeted; v0.1 remains on disk.

- **2026-08-09** — Layer 0 ratified. Three merges accepted (P-001 Baseline+Ablation→Model, P-002 Distribution→Prediction, P-003 SpreadCalibration+KappaCalibration→CostCalibration in `signals/proposals.json`). Six flags stamped inline and promoted into `signals/0.1.json § entities[]` (Bucket, MetricSnapshot, Split, Session, ExperimentConfig, Source). ExperimentConfig confirmed standalone (one-to-many with TrainingRun via `config_hash` foreign key; fold-into-TrainingRun rejected). Session confirmed Layer 0 + Layer 3 dual class. Layer 0 count: 31 entities. Step 2 (Layer 1 tags) opens.

*(further Decisions appended by the Architect as the project proceeds)*

## Built

*Agent appends one entry per sprint close. Append-only.*

- **Sprint 119 (2026-09-27)** — Held-out guard extended to token artifacts and the training entrypoint. `heldout_guard` gains `HELDOUT_NAME_MARK`, a pattern for bare `tokenize-features-align-YYYY-MM` output, and a symlink-target check; `scripts/train.py` refuses held-out input with exit 2 and no override. Five new guard tests; four fail against the old code (D3 check). The full suite then caught two train smokes that had trained on held-out tokens through `tokens.latest.pt` since Sprint 117; the four smokes now name the 2015-2022 artifact. README reproduce command names the training artifact. `pass_kind: functional`, `determinism_budget: bit-deterministic`.

- **Sprint 118 (2026-09-27)** — Baseline convergence probe and public correction. `scripts/analysis/baseline_convergence.py` sweeps lr x wd per baseline, early-stops on the transformer's 10,789 validation windows, re-runs the best config at 5 seeds. Linear at lr 1e-3 peaks at step 800 (3.2886) and overfits after; not undertrained. Tuned 5-seed means: linear 3.2556, GRU 3.2189, MLP 3.2120; transformer 3.1803 ahead by 2.31 / 1.20 / 0.99%. These are target-history baselines, not the spec's pre-registered ones (multi-channel linear and MLP, ~1M-param GRU/TCN, target-only ablation), so the pre-registered gates remain unmeasured. README, CHANGELOG, phase-h-close (appended correction + top pointer), successor spec opening, and `fit_*` docstrings corrected. Successor spec's first sprint renumbered 118 -> 120. `pass_kind: functional`, `determinism_budget: bit-deterministic`.

- **Sprint 117 (2026-08-26)** — Phase H close and successor spec. Ran the held-out simulator against the Sprint 115 mixer on the untouched 2024-01 through 2025-06 SPY window (10,166 bars, 1 basis point spread + 0.5 bp slippage at SPY norms): Sharpe -1.82 to -2.17 across three thresholds; Sharpe minus SE fails at every one; bootstrap positive-fraction ~0.001. The log-loss edge from Phase H does not translate to a directional trading edge. Simulator scale bug found and fixed inline at phase open: `derive_prediction_scalars` produced a raw log-return under a name promising a vol-normalized one; `compute_edge` treated it as vol-normalized; the two scales differed by ~600x on SPY 15-min bars, so the pre-fix simulator produced one trade across three years. Post-mortem at `postmortems/simulator-scale.md`. Six existing tests updated; full suite 629 pass. Docs reorganized: `postmortems/` and `reports/` split out of `reviews/`. Standard project files added (README, LICENSE, SECURITY, CHANGELOG); repo renamed to `large-price-model` on GitHub. Successor spec drafted at `specs/large-price-model-v1.md`. Held-out look log at 2 of 3 spent. `pass_kind: functional`, `determinism_budget: bit-deterministic`.

- **Sprint 116 (2026-08-25)** — Multi-seed mixer lr=3e-5 (5 seeds, seed 0 from Sprint 115 + seeds 1-4 new). Purpose: check whether Sprint 115's "+4.26% below linear" was seed-lucky. Result: pooled val_nll mean **3.180 ± 0.022** across 5 seeds; mean margin **+3.29%** below linear (vs Sprint 114 sum-multiseed's +1.87%). Every seed beats linear. Tightness: stdev 0.022 nats vs sum's 0.056 — mixer both better on average and more consistent. But: 3/5 seeds diverge before step 5060 (grad_norm > 10). Top-K checkpointing preserved best-pre-divergence weights so numbers hold; regime sits on stability edge. Sprint 115's +4.26% seed 0 was near the top of the distribution but not extreme. Bugs caught + fixed inline: trainer's checkpoint config was omitting `fusion`/`mixer_dim`/`mixer_n_heads` (Sprint 083 mixer never round-tripped through evaluate.py); `evaluate.py::_load_market_state_checkpoint` didn't thread those fields. Fixed both at source; backfilled 6 existing mixer checkpoints on disk. Logbook: 26 rows. Report at `artifacts/sprint116-report.md`. `pass_kind: functional`, `determinism_budget: bit-deterministic`.

- **Sprint 115 (2026-08-25)** — Peak LR sweep + mixer retry, best arc result. 5 md-normalized runs at seed 0. Sum lr ∈ {5e-5, 3e-5, 1e-5} + mixer lr ∈ {3e-5, 1e-5}. Lower LR extends descent: best-checkpoint step moves from 2000 (Sprint 113/114 at lr=1e-4) to 8000 (lr=1e-5 sum, 3e-5 mixer) — the schedule-transition-overfitting diagnosed at Sprint 114 close is real and fixed by LR. **Best result of entire arc: mixer + lr=3e-5, pooled val_nll 3.148 (+4.26% vs linear), top1 10.9%, wins every regime cleanly** (low 2.84 vs linear 3.01, mid 3.28 vs 3.43, high 3.18 vs 3.31). Mixer at lr=1e-5 next best (+3.69%); sum lr=1e-5 +3.10%. Two bugs caught + fixed same sprint: trainer checkpoint-config missing `fusion` field; evaluate.py not threading it. Gap to gate cut from 7.2% to 5.7%. Multi-seed caveat noted, addressed at Sprint 116. Logbook: 21 rows. Report at `artifacts/sprint115-report.md`.

- **Sprint 114 (2026-08-25)** — Multi-seed sprint113-C + regularization sweep. 4 md-normalized seed runs (wd=0.1) + 3 wd runs (wd ∈ {0.5, 1.0, 5.0} at seed 0). **Multi-seed pooled val_nll mean 3.227, stdev 0.056, range [3.193, 3.326].** Mean margin vs linear +1.87%; range from -1.13% to +2.91%. Sprint 113's +2.77% headline was near top of the seed distribution. Top-1 much tighter (mean 10.07%, stdev 0.22%) — mode prediction stable, distribution calibration seed-sensitive. **Regularization dead end**: wd ∈ {0.1, 0.5, 1.0} produced essentially identical val_nll at step 2000; wd=5.0 diverged. **Real diagnosis: overfitting is a schedule-transition problem, not capacity or regularization.** Best-checkpoint consistently lands at end-of-warmup (step 1000-3000); peak LR at 1e-4 fine while ramping, kills training immediately after. Sprint 115 tests lower peak LR. Report at `artifacts/sprint114-report.md`.

- **Sprint 113 (2026-08-25)** — Feature-role probes surfaced the normalization bug. Sprint 113-A target-only on un-normalized artifact matched Sprint 110 v3 md to 4 decimals in every regime (n=1826/2629/6334; nll delta ≤ 0.0005) — proved multi-channel features contributed zero under sum fusion on un-normalized input. Sprint 113-B mixer probe blocked by Sprint 084's `UnnormalizedArtifactRefused` guard, which surfaced `normalized: False` in the artifact meta. Regenerated tokens via `scripts/bucketize.py --fit-normalizer` into `data/tokenized/normalized/`; **Sprint 113-C sum + normalized at md hit pooled val_nll 3.1976 at step 2000 vs linear@800's 3.289 = transformer +2.8% BETTER** — first arc win vs linear on every regime (low nll 2.9163 vs 3.006, mid 3.3234 vs 3.427, high 3.2264 vs 3.312) AND pooled AND top-1 (10.0% vs 8.8%). **Root cause**: raw `dollar_volume` scale (mean ~61M, std ~264M) dominated per-channel `nn.Linear(F_c, d_model)` projections inside sum fusion; non-target channels were noise-scaled artifacts contributing zero to the sum. Sprint 084's guard was scoped to mixer's visible divergence, not to sum's silent quality collapse; comment on the exception class explicitly claimed "sum fusion tolerates un-normalized input via internal LayerNorms and is unaffected" — untested and wrong. **Post-mortem at `reviews/post-mortem-normalization-bug.md`.** **All fixes landed same sprint, zero tech debt:** (1) `UnnormalizedArtifactRefused` broadened to refuse un-normalized under every fusion, error message names the Sprint 113 finding; (2) `data/tokenized/tokens.latest.pt` swapped to normalized version as canonical, un-normalized archived as `tokens.unnormalized.tokenize-features-align-2015-01-2022-12-...pt`; (3) `test_run_training_feats_permits_sum_on_unnormalized_artifact` replaced with `_refuses_` counterpart; (4) 4 fixture-based tests updated to stamp `normalized: True`; (5) `run_id` gains automatic `-hp{8char}` sha256 fingerprint of 15 training hyperparameters (lr, wd, warmup, lr_min_frac, bf16, embargo, keep_top_k, target_only, fusion, mixer_dim, mixer_n_heads, patch_size, head_type, batch_size, deterministic) so Sprint 112-style checkpoint collision is structurally impossible; (6) `artifacts/experiment-ledger.md` and `reviews/data-limitations-theorized.md` gained retraction headers. **Sprint 113-D mixer + normalized diverged at step 1688 (mid-warmup)** — cross-channel attention less stable than sum under lr=1e-4; retry candidate at lr ∈ {3e-5, 1e-5}. Sprint 113-C's normalized model overfits catastrophically after step 2000 (train loss drops 3.20 → 0.11, val_nll blows up 3.24 → 9.12); best checkpoint is end-of-warmup. Overfitting is now the arc's bottleneck; Sprint 114 candidate: regularization sweep (wd ∈ {0.5, 1.0, 5.0}) + longer schedule to stretch descent past step 2000. **Pre-registered 10% linear gate still fails** at 7.5% short (3.1976 vs threshold 2.960); direction now decisively correct, magnitude still needs training-stability work. **The Sprint 110-112 arc was diagnosing a normalization bug.** Every "transformer loses to linear" conclusion was correct against un-normalized transformers; retracted as claims about the multi-channel architecture. Sprint 111 review's central prediction ("multi-channel edge real but small") revised to "multi-channel edge real and beats linear once normalization applied." Test count unchanged (628 passed, 2 skipped); one test replaced, four fixtures updated, one new post-mortem, three retractions filed. Ruff/mypy/pytest all green. Files touched: `src/price_space_llm/model/trainer.py` (guard broaden + docstring retract), `scripts/train.py` (`--target-only` + `--run-id-tag` + automatic hp fingerprint), `tests/test_normalizer.py` + `tests/test_model.py` + `tests/test_trainer.py` (fixture/test updates), `reviews/post-mortem-normalization-bug.md` new, `reviews/data-limitations-theorized.md` retraction header, `artifacts/experiment-ledger.md` retraction header + Sprint 113 rows, `artifacts/sprint113-feature-role-report.md` new, `data/tokenized/tokens.latest.pt` swapped, canonical `tokens.unnormalized.*` archived. Logbook now at 10 rows. **Named on card:** Sprint 111 amended for the third time; Sprint 084's "sum tolerates un-normalized" claim retracted; every previous "transformer worse than linear" finding retracted; new correct headline: **transformer beats linear by 2.8% pooled on every regime under normalized artifact at just 2000 steps**. `pass_kind: functional`, `determinism_budget: bit-deterministic`.

- **Sprint 112 (2026-08-25)** — H1 vs H2 probe: does more training or more regularization close the linear-baseline gap? **H1 wins.** Two md runs side-by-side at seed=0. **md-longtrain** (100K steps planned, lr=1e-4, warmup=2000, wd=0.1) diverged at step 36907 (grad_norm 10.2 > 10) but hit best training-side val_nll 3.2133 at step 30000 — meaningfully better than Sprint 110 v3 md's 3.30. **md-regularized** (20K steps, wd=0.5, everything else identical to Sprint 110 v3 md) hit best training-side val_nll 3.2996 at step 12000 — essentially identical to Sprint 110 v3 md's 3.30. H2 rejected: weight decay 5× did nothing. Per-regime eval on md-longtrain@30000 vs linear baseline (linear pooled 3.3325): **low-vol** transformer 3.056 vs linear 3.135 (transformer wins +0.079 nats); **mid-vol** 3.351 vs 3.433 (transformer wins +0.082); **high-vol** 3.374 vs 3.348 (linear still wins +0.027 but by much less). **Pooled val_nll: transformer 3.3148 vs linear 3.3325 — transformer wins by 0.018 nats (+0.53%).** First run in the whole arc to beat linear on pooled. Reverses Sprint 111's "linear beats transformer everywhere" story: at 30K steps of training the transformer wins on 2 of 3 regimes and pooled, not the 0 of 3 at Sprint 110's 20K steps. Sprint 110's amended amendment: the transformer isn't broken, it isn't over-parameterized — it was under-trained. Multi-channel features aren't actively harmful; they're just slow to distill. **10% linear gate still misses by ~10%** (best pooled 3.315 vs gate threshold 3.00, which is 10% below linear's 3.333). Direction now correct, magnitude short by an order-of-magnitude of gate margin. **LR schedule is the actual bottleneck.** md-longtrain diverged because 100K-step cosine keeps LR near peak for a much longer fraction of the run than 20K-step cosine — at step 36K LR was still 7.5e-5 vs 1e-5 at step 20K under Sprint 110 v3's shorter schedule. Sprint 113 candidate: stable-schedule longtrain (peak LR 5e-5, or explicit flat-tail cosine, or linear decay, or grad clip 0.5) so training reaches 60-100K clean; plus lg at same longtrain config. **Tech debt caught: run_id naming collision.** Trainer builds run_id from `tokens+size+steps+seed` — Sprint 112 md-regularized used same size+steps+seed as Sprint 110 v3 md → same run_id → same checkpoint paths → Sprint 112 **overwrote Sprint 110's md checkpoints on disk**. Scientific loss small (val_nll numbers are ~identical) but reproducibility break is real. Fix: extend run_id to include config hash / hyperparameter fingerprint so distinct settings never collide. Sprint 113+ tech-debt slot. Files touched: none in `src/` (both probes are training runs via existing script); new artifacts: `logs/sprint112-md-{longtrain,regularized}/` traces, top-3 checkpoints per run, `artifacts/sprint112-md-{longtrain,regularized}-eval/metrics.json`, `artifacts/sprint112-h1-vs-h2-report.md`, 2 appended rows to `experiments/logbook.csv` via `register_from_trace.py`. Logbook now at 7 runs total. Ruff/mypy/pytest untouched. **Named on card:** Sprint 111's "H2 signature from noisy trajectory tail" was wrong; the noise is schedule noise, not overfit noise. `pass_kind: functional`, `determinism_budget: bit-deterministic`.

- **Sprint 111 (2026-08-25)** — Linear per-regime diagnostic. Answered a single question that Sprint 110 left open: does linear beat the transformer everywhere, or only on high-vol which drags the pooled? **Answer: linear beats the transformer on every regime.** Built `scripts/analysis/linear_per_regime.py` — fits `LinearBaseline(64×32→32)` at 200 Adam steps on training-window bucket-IDs, evaluates on val, bins by the same 33rd/66th percentile regime thresholds `evaluate.py` uses (fit from `target__SPY__realized_vol_30` on the training partition). Handles the -100 masked-invalid sentinel by clamp-to-zero in inputs + valid-target filter on outputs. Pooled result at n_steps=200: val_nll 3.3325 — matches `baselines.py`'s 3.3331 to four decimal places, confirms the diagnostic path is correct. Diagnostic side-finding: at n_steps=5000 the linear baseline **overfits** (pooled val_nll 3.587, 0.25 nats worse); 200 steps is the right budget for a 65K-param linear on 43K training rows. **Real per-regime comparison** (linear vs md transformer): low-vol linear nll 3.135 vs md 3.182 (linear wins nll by 0.05, md wins top-1 16.0% vs 14.1%); mid-vol linear 3.433 vs md 3.416 (md wins by 0.02 nats); **high-vol linear 3.348 vs md 3.466 (linear wins by 0.12 nats)**. High-vol dominates val n (6614 of 10789 = 61%); it's where linear's pooled advantage lives. Top-1: linear high-vol 7.6% vs md 2.6% — linear delivers 3× better prediction on the largest val bucket while md sits below chance-of-random (3.1%). Directional accuracy: linear beats md in every regime (55.0/52.0/51.7% vs 53.1/49.3/49.2%); md's dir-acc is below coin-flip in mid/high. **Sprint 110's story "high-vol has no learnable signal at 15-min horizon" was wrong.** The signal exists; the transformer just isn't finding it. Two live hypotheses for the transformer's regime-wide loss: H1 (under-training — 20K steps at lr=1e-4 not enough for 14M params to distill even the auto-regressive bucket structure) and H2 (overfitting on cross-channel noise — 14M params + 43K rows + 20 mostly-sparse channels over-parameterizes; cross-channel memorization hurts generalization). Signal favoring H2: md's val_nll saturated at step 12000 then oscillated 3.28-3.44 for 8000 more steps — flat with high noise is a regularization signature, not a training-length signature. Sprint 112 tests both side by side. **Tracking discipline fix:** `experiments/logbook.csv` didn't exist before this sprint. Every Sprint 110 run + this sprint's linear-baseline run retroactively registered (5 rows total: xs, sm, md, lg, sprint111-linear). New helper `scripts/register_from_trace.py` automates registration for future runs by reading a training trace + optional evaluate metrics.json and shelling out to `scripts/register_run.py`. Files touched: `scripts/analysis/linear_per_regime.py` (new); `scripts/register_from_trace.py` (new); `sprints/sprint-111-linear-per-regime-diagnostic.md` (new); `artifacts/sprint111-linear-per-regime.json` (new); `artifacts/sprint111-comparison-report.md` (new); `experiments/logbook.csv` (5 appended rows, first-write). Ruff green; mypy skipped for `scripts/analysis/` per project mypy config. **Named on card:** the "transformer wins big on low-vol" claim from Sprint 110 report is amended — the transformer wins on low-vol *against chance*, but linear does even better on low-vol. Sprint 112 opens next: md-longtrain (100K steps, wd=0.1) vs md-regularized (20K steps, wd=0.5), both at seed=0 for cross-run comparability; H1 vs H2 verdict falls out of the two. `pass_kind: functional`, `determinism_budget: bit-deterministic`.

- **Sprint 110 (2026-08-24)** — Model-size sweep on M5 Max via MPS (roadmap 084). First hard training numbers Phase H produced against the 2015-2022 corpus. Sweep at four sizes (xs 819K, sm 2.7M, md 14.3M, lg 25.3M) × two LR settings: v2 at spec's `lr=3e-4` diverged at bigger sizes (grad_norm > 10 divergence-detector fired at steps 17851/12267/9217 for sm/md/lg; xs completed clean), classic evidence LR too high for larger transformers on this data; v3 at `lr=1e-4` all four ran clean through 20K steps. Reported sweep is v3. Wall-clock ~74 min sequential (xs 10, sm 13, md 22, lg 29). **Pooled val_nll** (evaluate.py weighted per regime, three regimes low/mid/high vol at n=1826/2629/6334): xs 3.4624, sm 3.4284, md 3.4060, lg 3.3993. Monotonic scaling with diminishing returns: xs→sm gains 0.034 nats, sm→md 0.022, md→lg 0.007. md is the winning size on cost/benefit for this feature set + training budget. **Baseline comparison on the same 2015-2022 corpus:** linear (Linear(64×32→32) on past 64 one-hot bucket-IDs, target-only auto-regression) val_nll = **3.3331**; target_only (marginal-frequency, meaningless per Sprint 041) = 3.4798; magnitude-weighted = 3.6159; chance log(32) = 3.4657. Pre-registered gates against best-size (lg): (a) "val_nll ≥10% lower than linear" **FAILS** by 1.99% — the transformer is *worse* than a 64-token linear regression on bucket-IDs; (b) "≥5% lower than target-only" fails but the gate itself is broken per Sprint 041 review; (c) "≥5% lower than magnitude-weighted" **PASSES** for sm/md/lg. **Per-regime breakdown changes the story:** the transformer wins big on low-vol (lg low-vol nll 3.155 vs chance 3.466 = 9% below chance; top-1 16.0% vs chance 3.1% = 5× chance; xs low-vol dir-acc 60% vs chance 50%) and does essentially nothing on high-vol (top-1 ≈ 2.6%, dir-acc ≈ 49%). Pooled val_nll gets dragged down by high-vol which dominates n_examples (6334 of 10789 val rows) but carries no learnable signal at 15-min horizon. Two possible reads on the linear-beats-transformer finding: (a) under-training — 20K steps at lr=1e-4 may not be enough for the transformer to distill multi-channel signal, longer training may close the gap; (b) signal ceiling — 15-min bar returns are dominated by autocorrelation + noise, cross-asset/macro/options/event features may not add value at this horizon. Report at `artifacts/sprint110-sweep-report.md` (5 findings, per-regime tables, gate results). Files touched: 4 code files unchanged (only the training script was invoked); 2 new sprint artifacts (sweep report, per-size eval metrics.json under `artifacts/sprint110-{size}-eval/`); 16 new checkpoint files (top-3 per size, 4 sizes); 8 trace files (`logs/sprint110-sweep-{size}` v3 + `logs/sprint110-sweep-{size}-lr3e4` v2). **Named on card:** the pooled-val-NLL pre-registered gate may be the wrong metric on this data because high-vol dominates n_examples but carries no signal. Sprint 111 candidate work: (a) diagnostic — run linear per-regime to see whether the transformer's low-vol advantage over linear is real; (b) longer training run at md (100K steps) to see if the linear gap closes; (c) if the low-vol edge holds vs linear, propose an amendment to the pre-registered gate framing to regime-conditional. `pass_kind: functional`, `determinism_budget: bit-deterministic`.

- **Sprint 109 (2026-08-24)** — First GPU rental blocked, three repo bugs caught, pivoted to local MPS after the operator named the actual compute available. Cloud path started per roadmap 083 (rent one `g5.xlarge` spot, run one xs=1M end-to-end training against the 2015-2022 corpus, verify parity with local CPU numbers). Ran into two AWS blocks in sequence: (a) new accounts lack the `AWSServiceRoleForEC2Spot` service-linked role — fixed under root with `aws iam create-service-linked-role --aws-service-name spot.amazonaws.com`, one-time on this account; (b) new accounts start with zero vCPU quota for both spot (`L-3819A6DF`) and on-demand (`L-DB2E81BA`) GPU instances — quota bump requests submitted to 4 vCPU each, await async AWS approval (typically hours to days). Rather than wait, ran three t3.medium CPU dry-runs to smoke the boot-script pipeline. Each caught a distinct bug: (1) `sync_to_gpu.sh` wrote the manifest with repo-relative paths (`data/tokenized/tokens.latest.pt`) but uploaded files at basename, so `sha256sum -c` on the flat S3 download failed every entry — fixed by computing hashes inside each file's dir with `basename`; (2) the code tarball omitted `sdd-kit-2/lib/sdd.py` which `pyproject.toml`'s wheel force-includes, so remote `uv sync` failed `FileNotFoundError: Forced include not found` — fixed by extending the tarball file list to `sdd-kit-2/lib` + `signals/`; (3) on-demand `shutdown -h now` **stops** the instance, does not terminate — EBS keeps costing pennies indefinitely; fixed by adding `InstanceInitiatedShutdownBehavior=terminate` to the RunInstances JSON. Every fix pinned by a regression test. After all three fixes the CPU pipeline runs cleanly through payload download → manifest verify → code unpack → uv install → uv sync → package build (`price-space-llm==0.15.0`) → torch install → the intentional `AssertionError: no CUDA` at the GPU probe. Trap fires, FAIL marker + provision log upload, box terminates. Wall-clock: ~2m30s per attempt. Live AWS spend across all three dry-runs plus lingering-stopped-instance storage until termination: under $0.05. **Pivot:** the operator asked whether the M5 Max could run the training. Verified hardware: MacBook Pro Mac17,7, Apple M5 Max, 18 CPU cores (6 efficiency + 12 performance), **40 GPU cores**, 128 GB unified memory, Metal 4, macOS 26.5.1. Ran cpu-vs-mps bench across xs/sm/md/lg (50 steps each): **bit-identical `final_train_loss` at every size** — 3.5182 (xs), 3.6494 (sm), 3.5005 (md), 3.5688 (lg). Cross-device determinism preserved. MPS starts winning at md (1.38×) and lg (1.57×); below md the kernel-launch overhead dominates. For 10K real steps at lg, MPS finishes in ~14 min vs CPU ~22 min. Full v1 workload (~20 runs across the four sweeps) fits in an afternoon on this box. **Conclusion:** cloud rental adds no throughput v1 asks for. The AWS scaffolding built in Sprints 107-108 stays warm as a scale-up option for v2 or a comparative sweep, but day-to-day training moves to `--device mps` on the M5 Max. Runbook `docs/runbooks/aws-infrastructure.md` reframed accordingly: header adds "Role in v1: **not the primary training environment.** Kept warm as a scale-up option," §3 renamed "Cloud training flow (used only if a scale-up trigger fires)," Purpose section added naming the M5 Max as primary and enumerating the three concrete scale-up triggers (v2 at ~300M+ params; parallel-seed sweeps; explicit CUDA-machine reproduction). Two open GPU quota tickets left in place at zero cost. Tests +3: `test_sync_manifest_uses_basenames_only`, `test_sync_code_tarball_includes_sdd_kit_and_signals`, `test_provision_json_includes_instance_initiated_shutdown_terminate`. Test count 625 → 628. Files touched: `scripts/aws/provision_gpu.sh` (5 edits — run_id arg, boot-script rewrite, terminate on shutdown, terminology switch from "user data" to "boot script" per operator preference, env-var overrides for one-off launches); `scripts/aws/sync_to_gpu.sh` (2 edits — basename manifest, sdd-kit + signals in tarball); 2 new tests + regression assertions in existing tests; `docs/runbooks/aws-infrastructure.md` framing rewrite; Sprint 109 card execution log. Ruff, mypy, pytest all green. **Lesson filed:** the operator called out the overcomplication honestly; two sprints of AWS scaffolding for a workload the laptop already handles. Not wasted — infrastructure is real, tested, and cheap to keep — but out of proportion. The right question at the top of Phase H would have been "what does the workload actually need?" not "how do we rent an A100?" Filed as KIT_DIARY-style lesson: *before ordering hardware, size the hardware the workload calls for.* Sprint 110 opens next: model-size sweep (roadmap 084), 4 runs at xs/sm/md/lg, `--device mps`, full training budget, first real curves against pre-registered gates. `pass_kind: functional`, `determinism_budget: bit-deterministic`.

- **Sprint 108 (2026-08-24)** — AWS pre-flight tightening from review. Every finding in `reviews/aws-and-phase-g-review.md` §12 addressed: five blockers + four should-fixes + one non-blocking `shutdown -h` correction + two runbook notes. **New AWS resources created live:** IAM role `pslm-v1-ec2-training` (root; scoped user cannot create roles per `DenyIAMEscalation`) with inline role policy `EC2TrainingRole` from `scripts/aws/instance-role-policy.json` granting the running EC2 box `s3:Get/PutObject/ListBucket` on the two buckets and `logs:Create/Put/DescribeStreams` on the CloudWatch log group; IAM instance profile with the same name (root) wrapping the role for EC2 attachment; dedicated security group `pslm-v1-training-sg` (pslm-v1) in the default VPC with zero ingress rules and one egress rule 443/TCP → 0.0.0.0/0 (S3, Alpha-Vantage, package repos over HTTPS); AWS-side managed-policy `PriceSpaceLLMV1` published as **v3** default (v1 initial, v2 added `budgets:ModifyBudget`, v3 this sprint). **Policy v3 changes** (all against `scripts/aws/iam-policy.json`): (1) new `DenyS3DeleteBucket` statement scoped to the two named bucket ARNs; a misfired teardown script cannot delete production data because Deny beats Allow in AWS's evaluation. (2) removed `iam:PassRole` from `DenyIAMEscalation`; added it back as `PassInstanceProfileRoleOnly` scoped to `arn:aws:iam::648879824221:role/pslm-v1-ec2-training` with a `StringEquals iam:PassedToService = ec2.amazonaws.com` condition — narrow-scope pass-role for the instance profile only. (3) split the Sprint 107 `CloudWatchAndLogs` statement (which mixed a specific log-group ARN with `"*"` in the Resource list, making the ARN meaningless) into three statements: `CloudWatchMetrics` on `Resource: "*"` (metrics do not accept ARN scoping), `CloudWatchLogs` scoped to the log-group ARN, `CloudWatchLogsList` for `logs:DescribeLogGroups` on `"*"` (list ops need wildcard). (4) added `ec2:CreateSecurityGroup`, `ec2:AuthorizeSecurityGroupEgress`, `ec2:RevokeSecurityGroupEgress`, `ec2:DeleteSecurityGroup` to the EC2 Allow list. **`provision_gpu.sh` wiring:** three new fields on the RunInstances JSON — `MetadataOptions: {HttpTokens: required, HttpEndpoint: enabled}` (IMDSv2 forced, IMDSv1 SSRF surface closed per review §11.1); `IamInstanceProfile: {Name: "pslm-v1-ec2-training"}`; `SecurityGroupIds: [<resolved-by-name>]`. Security-group name is resolved at launch via `aws ec2 describe-security-groups --group-names` so callers do not need to hard-code the SG ID; on `--dry-run` when the SG does not yet exist a `sg-DRYRUN-PLACEHOLDER` is emitted so the JSON stays valid + inspectable. Walltime shutdown gains explicit `-h` flag per review §11.2. **`sync_to_gpu.sh`:** one-line `sha256sum` → `shasum -a 256` fallback shim so the same script runs on both Linux and macOS. **Rename per review §11.5:** `SPRINT_107_AUTHORIZED_RUN` → `PSLM_AUTHORIZED_GPU_RUN` (sprint-numbered env vars accrue and confuse future readers; the semantic is "authorized GPU training run," not "authorized Sprint 107 thing"). Rename touches `scripts/train.py`, `tests/test_train_ec2_guard.py`, and `docs/runbooks/aws-infrastructure.md`. Tests +8: four IAM-policy-shape assertions pinning the review fixes (`DenyS3DeleteBucket` exists on both buckets; no statement mixes specific log-group ARN with `*`; `iam:PassRole` is `Allow`-scoped to the training role ARN and not in Deny; SG create/authorize actions present in the policy; policy size under 6144-byte managed-policy limit); one provision-JSON assertion verifying MetadataOptions + IamInstanceProfile + SecurityGroupIds all populated; one shell-level test proving the `sha256sum` shim runs on a PATH without Linux `sha256sum`; one defensive rename test proving `SPRINT_107_AUTHORIZED_RUN=1` (stale name) does NOT bypass the guard. Test count 615 → 623. Live-verified: dry-run against the actual AWS resources emits a fully-populated RunInstances request with `sg-001faab872e311c38` (real SG ID), IMDSv2 enforced, instance profile attached. Files touched: 4 code edits (`iam-policy.json`, `provision_gpu.sh`, `sync_to_gpu.sh`, `train.py`) + 1 new file (`instance-role-policy.json`) + 3 test files (1 new + 2 edits) + 1 runbook edit. Ruff, mypy, pytest all green. **Runbook additions:** new §2.4a (IAM role + instance profile creation, under root), new §2.4b (security group creation, under pslm-v1), new §3a (cost-coverage caveat: tag-filtered budgets cover EC2 compute only; EBS/S3/CloudWatch/data-transfer land in an untagged bucket), §4 note about `DeleteOnTermination=true` assumption on root EBS. Sprint 109 opens next: rent the first `g5.xlarge` spot instance, sync the payload, run one xs=1M end-to-end training run against the 2015-2022 corpus, verify parity with local CPU numbers within numerical noise. `pass_kind: architecture`, `determinism_budget: bit-deterministic`.

- **Sprint 107 (2026-08-24)** — AWS GPU pre-provisioning scaffolding (roadmap 083 prep). Every resource that must exist before Sprint 108 boots an EC2 instance now lives in account `648879824221` (awsPro), region us-east-1, under scoped IAM user `price-space-llm-v1` — root credentials were never used for anything but the initial user creation. `docs/runbooks/aws-infrastructure.md` is the single source of truth: inventory of every AWS resource, first-time provisioning commands, day-to-day flow, failure modes, and end-of-v1 teardown. **Resources created live:** IAM user `price-space-llm-v1` + customer-managed policy `PriceSpaceLLMV1` (3735 bytes; the 2048-byte inline-policy limit forced the managed-policy path); two S3 buckets `price-space-llm-v1-{payload,logs}` with versioning ON, public-access-block on all four flags, AES-256 default encryption; SNS topic `price-space-llm-v1-alerts` with pending email subscription to `laffeypeter@gmail.com`; three Budgets `pslm-v1-{500,1500,3000}` tag-filtered to `Project=price-space-llm-v1`, SNS-notified at 100% actual + 90% forecast. **Advisory-only alarms** — no `RunInstances` stop action wired; a breach informs, it does not halt. **IAM policy shape:** two `Deny` statements bound the blast radius (`RegionLock` forbids every action outside us-east-1 except a small whitelist; `DenyIAMEscalation` explicitly forbids `iam:CreateUser / AttachUserPolicy / PutUserPolicy / CreateAccessKey / CreateRole / AttachRolePolicy / PutRolePolicy / PassRole`), followed by `Allow` statements for EC2 compute, S3 (scoped to the two named buckets only), CloudWatch/Logs, Budgets create+read+delete, SNS create+subscribe+publish, and self-identity. **Two policy gaps caught during live provisioning:** `sns:CreateTopic` was missing from the first draft; `budgets:CreateBudget` internally checks `budgets:ModifyBudget` which was also missing. Both fixed; the runbook change-log records the pattern. **Repo code:** `scripts/aws/provision_gpu.sh` (dev = `g5.xlarge` spot + 2hr walltime, prod = `p4d.24xlarge` on-demand + no cap, `--dry-run` flag prints the RunInstances JSON without firing the API, `--fallback` switches instance type if p4d quota not approved); `scripts/aws/sync_to_gpu.sh` (packages tokens.latest.pt + normalizers.latest.pt + bucket_stats.latest.json + code tarball; deterministic SHA-256 manifest via sorted `find | LC_ALL=C sort` then `tar -T | gzip -n` because BSD tar lacks `--sort/--owner/--mtime`); `scripts/aws/teardown_gpu.sh` (by instance-id or by `RunSprint=<id>` tag filter, idempotent, cleans orphaned EBS + open spot requests). **Training-script EC2 guard:** `scripts/train.py::_enforce_ec2_authorization_guard()` invoked at top of `main()` refuses to run on EC2 (detected via IMDSv2 or `AWS_EC2_INSTANCE_ID` env var) unless `SPRINT_107_AUTHORIZED_RUN=1` is set. Prevents accidental wall-time burn on a still-alive rented box. `PSLM_SKIP_EC2_CHECK=1` bypass for tests. Tests +11: five provisioning-CLI tests (dev dry-run emits g5.xlarge spot request; prod dry-run emits on-demand request; --fallback switches instance type; missing mode returns exit 2; SPRINT_ID env override lands in RunSprint tag); three sync-manifest tests (all payload files listed in manifest; deterministic across runs given identical tree; --run-id required); three EC2-guard tests (faked EC2 metadata without auth env → exit 1 + fix-path message; with auth env → exit 0; off EC2 → guard is a no-op). Test count 604 → 615. Live-verified: `aws sts get-caller-identity --profile pslm-v1` returns the scoped user ARN; `aws s3 ls` lists both buckets; `aws budgets describe-budgets` lists all three; `aws sns list-subscriptions-by-topic` shows the pending email confirmation. Files touched: 6 code (`scripts/aws/{iam-policy.json, budgets.json, provision_gpu.sh, sync_to_gpu.sh, teardown_gpu.sh}` new; `scripts/train.py` edit) + 3 test files (new) + 1 runbook (new, `docs/runbooks/aws-infrastructure.md`). Ruff, mypy, pytest all green. **Named on card:** root access key deletion is a manual operator step (the Agent cannot rotate the credential it's currently authenticated with mid-session); the scoped `pslm-v1` profile is now the day-to-day credential. **One secret exposure:** `~/.aws/credentials` reading showed the pslm-v1 secret access key in the session transcript during the install-key step. Not a leak to third parties; the transcript stays in the user's own session boundary. Documented so the operator can decide to rotate the key. Sprint 108 opens next: rent a `g5.xlarge` spot instance, sync the payload, run one xs=1M end-to-end training run, verify metrics match local CPU numbers within numerical noise. `pass_kind: architecture`, `determinism_budget: bit-deterministic`.

- **Sprint 106 (2026-08-24)** — Phase G audit + commit-msg hook install + shell-wrapper bugfix. Roadmap items 079-082 all live in `HEAD` and were verified against tech-arch §13; the errata block's claim that each item landed bundled into an earlier sprint checked out. `scripts/register_run.py` writes a 22-column `experiments/logbook.csv` on demand (`append_run` calls `path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)` before writing; the directory does not sit idle on disk, which is a design choice). `src/price_space_llm/heldout_guard.py` + `scripts/evaluate.py`'s `--i-know-this-is-a-test-look` acknowledgment wire the held-out filesystem guard. `tests/test_spec_guards.py` carries the no-hardcoded-target grep and the channel-source-of-truth pt-vs-parquet byte-identity test. **Drift #1 caught + fixed:** `scripts/check_test_look.sh` existed but `.git/hooks/commit-msg` was never symlinked, so the pre-commit test-look guard never fired on any commit in this repo's history. Fix: `ln -sf "$(pwd)/scripts/check_test_look.sh" .git/hooks/commit-msg`; symlink noted on the sprint card because it does not travel with clones. **Drift #2 caught + fixed:** the Sprint 064 shell wrapper contained the line `"$PYTHON" - "$COMMIT_MSG_FILE" <<'PYEOF' <<<"$STAGED_FILES"`, which uses two competing stdin redirections; bash keeps only the last, so the heredoc Python script never reached the interpreter — the Python process instead read the staged-files list as its source code and died with `NameError: name 'configs' is not defined`. The pure-Python `check_commit_for_test_look` had test coverage; the shell wrapper around it did not. Rewrote the wrapper to pipe `git diff --cached --name-only` on stdin and pass the commit-message path via argv. Live-verified: `configs/audit/heldout_probe.json` carrying `"2024-06-30"` + innocent commit message → hook exits 1 with "BLOCKING commit. staged config files reference dates >= 2024-01-01 ..."; same file + `[test-look]` in message → hook exits 0. - **Sprint 103 (2026-08-19)** — Phase-G perf cleanup (review §3.1 + §3.3 + §3.4 + §2.68). Four mechanical accumulator swaps + type tightening; no behavior change. §3.1: `derive_prices_from_log_returns` becomes `torch.where(isfinite, r, 0)` → `cumsum` → `exp` in three lines. Semantically identical to the Sprint 096 loop — NaN log-return means zero log-return means price stays flat between bars. Pinned bit-tight against retained loop reference over 100-bar mixed-NaN input. §3.3: `build_equity_curve` uses `np.searchsorted(sorted_bar_ts, exit_epochs, side="right")` in one call per branch instead of per-trade `bisect_right` iteration. Mypy fought the return-type overload of `np.searchsorted` (it thinks the scalar-input overload wins); resolved via `np.asarray(..., dtype=np.int64)` cast. §3.4: `BucketStats.train_means_tensor` becomes a `@property` — cached-property machinery fights `frozen=True + slots=True` (would need `__slots__` cache attribute + `object.__setattr__` bypass, both wrong); plain property with construct-once contract is honest. Walker call sites (three) read `bucket_stats.train_means_tensor` instead of building the tensor manually. §2.68: `SimResult.notes` typed as `Literal["skeleton", "predictions-only", "predictions+policy", "predictions+policy+trades"]`; discriminator now checkable at mypy time. Tests +5: vectorized-prices bit-tight vs Sprint 096 loop over 100-bar mixed-NaN; first-bar-holds-running-at-exp semantics on finite + NaN heads; `train_means_tensor` returns correct shape and values on a hand-computed 5-bucket stats; searchsorted matches hand-computed 3-trade 10-bar ledger; notes accepts the four declared Literal values. Test count 600 → 605. Live smoke on real corpus: identical every-field mark-to-market metrics as Sprint 100/102.
