### Sprint 117 (2026-08-26, closed) — Phase H close, held-out result, successor spec

- **Scope:** run the held-out simulator against the best trained model (Sprint 115 mixer, val_nll 3.148) and record the number that closes the phase. Fix any simulator bug found along the way. Reorganize docs. Publish. Draft the successor spec.
- **Dual contract:** signal (unchanged — the walker emits every declared tag; look-log registration fires `TEST_LOOK_REGISTERED`) + artifact (holdout tokens regenerated with the frozen normalizer applied; six existing prediction-scalar tests updated to the fixed invariant; three new docs — README, LICENSE, SECURITY, CHANGELOG; two post-mortems; two folders — `postmortems/`, `reports/`; one successor spec at `specs/large-price-model-v1.md`; full suite 629 pass).
- **Observation contract:** `pass_kind: functional`, `determinism_budget: bit-deterministic`. Held-out live-smoke on the untouched 2024-01 through 2025-06 SPY window at 10,166 bars, three policy thresholds (0.005, 0.020, 0.050): Sharpe = -1.82, -2.17, -2.02; Sharpe SE = 0.81, 0.66, 0.64; Sharpe minus SE = -2.62, -2.84, -2.66; bootstrap positive-fraction ~0.001 at every threshold; ~900 trades each. Training-window val-slice smoke on the fixed simulator: 913-1102 trades across the sweep, Sharpe -0.49 to -1.20, comparable directional-verdict to the held-out result.
- **Rubber Duck Pass:**
  - *Observation 1 (silent scale mismatch worse than a crash).* The simulator ran clean for weeks with random-weight checkpoints — the scale mismatch didn't manifest because random weights produced roughly balanced edges around zero. Only a trained model, whose mean prediction is genuinely near zero, exposed the scale problem: cost terms swamped the signal. Filed to `postmortems/simulator-scale.md`.
  - *Observation 2 (end-to-end run before phase close catches interpretation bugs).* No trained model had ever been fed to the simulator until phase close. Every prior smoke used a step-8 xs run. The bug had been latent since Sprints 093-094 (August 17). Lesson filed to KIT_DIARY: any downstream component needs an end-to-end run against a real upstream artifact at least once before the phase closes, or the interpretation of its numbers is unfounded.
  - *Observation 3 (the finding stands regardless).* The fixed simulator's directional-strategy Sharpe is negative on both training-window val and held-out data. The transformer's log-loss edge is in the distribution's shape, not the mean. A directional decoder reads only the mean and captures none of it. The result is consistent across the fix.
  - *Observation 4 (doc reorganization).* `postmortems/` and `reports/` split out of `reviews/` as top-level folders. `reviews/` now holds only reviews.
  - *Observations 5-6 (missing pair / timing / tone).* None.
- **Files:** `src/price_space_llm/simulation/prediction.py` (fix + docstring + fail-loud vol guard); `tests/test_simulation_prediction.py` (six tests updated); `README.md`, `LICENSE`, `SECURITY.md`, `CHANGELOG.md` (new, docs); `postmortems/simulator-scale.md`, `postmortems/normalization-bug.md` (moved from reviews/, new post-mortem); `reports/phase-h-close.md`, `reports/phase-f-close.md` (moved from reviews/); `specs/large-price-model-v1.md` (new). Also a scratchpad simulator-run script and a held-out driver script (outside git). Two commits pushed: `9352062` (phase close), `7ae4257` and successors (docs cleanup). Repo renamed to `large-price-model` on GitHub. Held-out look log at 2 of 3 spent; one remains.
- **Closed:** clean. Initial phase (A through H) closed. Successor project opens on Sprint 118 (database design as a document).

### Sprint 116 (2026-08-25, closed) — Multi-seed mixer, +3.29% mean margin, stability edge

- **Scope:** 4 new seeds of the sprint115-mixer-3e-5 config to check whether +4.26% was seed-lucky.
- **Result:** 5-seed pooled mean 3.180, stdev 0.022, mean margin +3.29% vs linear. Every seed beats linear. Tighter than sum multi-seed (Sprint 114 stdev 0.056). Sprint 115's +4.26% was near top of distribution.
- **Stability problem:** 3/5 seeds diverge before step 5060. Top-K checkpointing preserves best pre-divergence weights so val_nll numbers hold.
- **Bugs caught inline:** trainer checkpoint config was omitting fusion/mixer_dim/mixer_n_heads. Sprint 083 mixer support never round-tripped through evaluate.py. Fixed at source + backfilled 6 existing checkpoints.
- **Closed:** clean. Mixer beats sum both on average and on tightness, but on the edge of stability.

### Sprint 115 (2026-08-25, closed) — Best arc result: mixer + lr=3e-5, +4.26%

- **Scope:** peak LR sweep on sum (5e-5, 3e-5, 1e-5) + mixer retry (3e-5, 1e-5).
- **Result:** mixer + lr=3e-5, pooled val_nll 3.148, +4.26% vs linear. Wins every regime, top-1 10.9%. Best of arc.
- **Diagnosis confirmed:** overfitting-at-warmup-end was a peak-LR problem. Lower LR moves best-checkpoint from step 2000 to step 8000-12000.
- **Bugs caught inline:** trainer checkpoint config missing `fusion`; evaluate.py hardcoded fusion=sum default. Both fixed at source.
- **Closed:** with a headline (subject to Sprint 116 multi-seed check).

### Sprint 114 (2026-08-25, closed) — Multi-seed sprint113-C + regularization sweep

- **Scope:** 4 seeds of Sprint 113-C config (wd=0.1) + 3 wd values at seed 0.
- **Result:** 5-seed pooled mean +1.87% vs linear (Sprint 113's +2.77% was near top). Top-1 stable across seeds (mean 10.07% ± 0.22%). wd ∈ {0.1, 0.5, 1.0} identical val_nll; wd=5.0 diverged.
- **Diagnosis:** overfitting is a schedule-transition problem at end-of-warmup, not regularization. Peak LR too high for continued training.
- **Closed:** pointed Sprint 115 at peak LR.

### Sprint 113 (2026-08-25, closed) — Normalization bug discovered, all fixes landed same sprint

- **Scope:** target-only + mixer probes to test whether non-target channels add value. Both probes ran to completion in one sense — target-only proved channels contribute zero under un-normalized sum fusion, mixer's failure surfaced the normalization guard, follow-up sum-normalized run proved channels contribute a lot once normalization is applied.
- **Dual contract:** signal (unchanged; no new tags) + artifact (`UnnormalizedArtifactRefused` broadened, `--target-only` + `--run-id-tag` + auto hp fingerprint added to `scripts/train.py`, canonical `tokens.latest.pt` swapped to normalized, post-mortem + retraction headers filed, 628 tests pass).
- **Observation contract:** `pass_kind: functional`, `determinism_budget: bit-deterministic`. Live-verified: guard fires on `tokens.unnormalized.*.pt` under any fusion; Sprint 113-C normalized run beats linear 3.1976 vs 3.289 = +2.8%.
- **Rubber Duck Pass:**
  - *Observation 1 (silent quality collapse is worse than a crash).* Sprint 084's guard caught mixer's divergence symptom. It didn't catch sum's silent target-only-equivalence. Fail-loud guards should cover the failure class (any output quality drop), not the failure symptom (visible divergence). 71 days under the bug's shadow; caught in one hour once the target-only comparison was run.
  - *Observation 2 (untested assumption in a comment is a load-bearing lie).* Sprint 084's docstring line — *"sum fusion tolerates un-normalized input via internal LayerNorms and is unaffected"* — was based on no-divergence, not on validation performance. It read as authoritative and shaped every subsequent reader's mental model. Rewritten as retraction.
  - *Observation 3 (bit-identical ablations are diagnostic).* Sprint 113-A target-only matching Sprint 110 v3 md to 4 decimals in every regime was the tell. Sprint 111 review predicted "target-only a few percent worse than full." Bit-identical numbers should have provoked the "why exactly identical" question earlier. Filed as a KIT_DIARY-style lesson.
  - *Observation 4 (all debt fixed, not filed).* Operator directive was "no tech debt." All Sprint 113 fixes landed in the same sprint that surfaced the bug: guard broaden, canonical swap, test replacement, fixture updates, hp fingerprint. Post-mortem action list closes at 100%.
- **Files:** `src/price_space_llm/model/trainer.py` (guard broaden + docstring retract); `scripts/train.py` (--target-only + --run-id-tag + auto hp fingerprint); `tests/test_normalizer.py` (test replaced); `tests/test_model.py`, `tests/test_trainer.py` (fixture stamps); `reviews/post-mortem-normalization-bug.md` (new); `reviews/data-limitations-theorized.md` (retraction header); `artifacts/experiment-ledger.md` (retraction header + Sprint 113 rows); `artifacts/sprint113-feature-role-report.md` (new); `data/tokenized/tokens.latest.pt` (repointed); un-normalized archived.
- **Closed:** with a headline reversal and zero open debt. Sprint 114 candidate: regularization sweep on sum-normalized md (wd ∈ {0.5, 1.0, 5.0}) + mixer-normalized retry at lower LR.

### Sprint 112 (2026-08-25, closed) — H1 wins: transformer beats linear at 30K steps

- **Scope:** two md runs at seed=0, one testing under-training hypothesis (100K steps, wd=0.1), one testing overfitting hypothesis (20K steps, wd=0.5). Decide the direction Sprint 113 takes.
- **Dual contract:** signal (full training tag surface fires per run) + artifact (2 traces, top-3 checkpoints per run, 2 eval metrics.json, comparison report, 2 logbook rows).
- **Observation contract:** `pass_kind: functional`, `determinism_budget: bit-deterministic`. md-longtrain best pooled val_nll 3.3148 (+0.53% above linear 3.3325 — first arc win). md-regularized best pooled val_nll 3.4051 (no change from Sprint 110 v3). Longtrain diverged at step 36907 before reaching 100K.
- **Rubber Duck Pass:**
  - *Observation 1 (Sprint 111 amended a second time).* "The transformer isn't extracting even the auto-regressive bucket-ID signal linear captures" was true for md at 20K steps. It's not true for md at 30K steps. Same architecture, more training, different verdict.
  - *Observation 2 (schedule stability is the real bottleneck).* Same peak LR (1e-4) that ran clean to 20K exploded at 37K under a 100K-step schedule because cosine keeps LR near peak much longer. LR value alone doesn't predict stability — the interaction with schedule length does.
  - *Observation 3 (run_id collision).* Sprint 112 md-regularized overwrote Sprint 110 v3 md checkpoints at the same step numbers because run_id doesn't fingerprint hyperparameters. Reproducibility break; scientific loss zero because val_nll numbers matched by coincidence. Fix required.
  - *Observations 4-6 (missing pair / timing / tone).* None.
- **Files:** `logs/sprint112-md-{longtrain,regularized}/`, `artifacts/checkpoints/train-tokens.latest-md-s{100000,20000}-...`, `artifacts/sprint112-md-{longtrain,regularized}-eval/`, `artifacts/sprint112-h1-vs-h2-report.md`, `experiments/logbook.csv` (2 rows).
- **Closed:** with a training win in hand and a schedule bug to fix. Sprint 113 opens next: stable-schedule longtrain + lg-longtrain, then decide 1-min-corpus probe vs context-length sweep for Sprint 114.

### Sprint 111 (2026-08-25, closed) — Linear per-regime diagnostic

- **Scope:** did linear beat transformer only on high-vol (which drags pooled), or on every regime? Also close the tracking gap surfaced during Sprint 110 close.
- **Dual contract:** signal (unchanged) + artifact (`scripts/analysis/linear_per_regime.py` new; `scripts/register_from_trace.py` new; `experiments/logbook.csv` first-write with 5 retroactively-registered rows + 1 diagnostic row).
- **Observation contract:** `pass_kind: functional`, `determinism_budget: bit-deterministic`. Linear at n_steps=200 pooled 3.333 (matches baselines.py). Per-regime: linear wins nll everywhere, most decisively on high-vol (linear 3.348 vs md 3.466). Top-1: linear 7.6% on high-vol vs md 2.6% (below chance). Dir-acc: linear beats md everywhere; md sits below coin-flip on mid/high.
- **Rubber Duck Pass:**
  - *Observation 1 (Sprint 110's story wrong).* "Transformer wins big on low-vol" holds against chance but fails against linear. "High-vol has no learnable signal at 15-min horizon" is wrong — linear finds signal there; the transformer doesn't.
  - *Observation 2 (n_steps overfits linear).* 5000 steps → 3.587 vs 200 steps → 3.333. Small linear + small data + long training = overfitting. Right budget for linear = 200 steps.
  - *Observation 3 (tracking gap fixed retroactively).* `experiments/logbook.csv` didn't exist. Fixed by retroactive registration + `register_from_trace.py` helper for future runs.
  - *Observations 4-6 (missing pair / timing / tone).* None.
- **Files:** `scripts/analysis/linear_per_regime.py`, `scripts/register_from_trace.py`, `sprints/sprint-111-...md`, `artifacts/sprint111-{linear-per-regime.json,comparison-report.md}`, `experiments/logbook.csv`.
- **Closed:** clean. Sprint 112 opens next: md-longtrain (H1) vs md-regularized (H2).

### Sprint 110 (2026-08-24, closed) — Model-size sweep, first honest gate result

- **Scope:** four training runs at xs/sm/md/lg on M5 Max via MPS, full training budget, compare against pre-registered baselines.
- **Dual contract:** signal (full training tag surface fires per run: SESSION_INIT, CONFIG_RESOLVED, WINDOW_SAMPLED × 20000, TRAINING_STEP_COMPLETED × 20000, CHECKPOINT_WRITTEN × 20, EPOCH_COMPLETED, SESSION_COMPLETE) + artifact (16 checkpoints + 4 metrics.json + sweep report).
- **Observation contract:** `pass_kind: functional`, `determinism_budget: bit-deterministic`. Pooled val_nll: xs 3.4624, sm 3.4284, md 3.4060, lg 3.3993 — monotonic. Linear baseline: 3.3331. Transformer loses to linear on pooled by 2-4%.
- **Rubber Duck Pass:**
  - *Observation 1 (LR is data-dependent, not spec-fixed).* v2 at lr=3e-4 diverged for sm/md/lg with grad_norm > 10 at step-count that scaled inversely with model size. v3 at lr=1e-4 was stable. Spec's suggested `lr=3e-4` is wrong for this data at these model sizes.
  - *Observation 2 (pooled metric obscures real signal).* High-vol regime (n=6334, 59% of val) is essentially unlearnable at 15-min horizon; the model does chance-level top-1 there. Low-vol (n=1826, 17%) is where the model wins big (5× chance top-1). The weighted-pooled val_nll drags the good number toward the bad, hiding the real edge.
  - *Observation 3 (linear baseline is competent).* `Linear(64×32→32)` on past 64 one-hot bucket-IDs is not a strawman. It's 3.33 vs transformer 3.40. Auto-regressive bucket structure at 15-min bars carries real signal on its own.
  - *Observation 4 (xs beats bigger models on low-vol dir-acc).* xs low-vol dir-acc 60% vs sm/md/lg 53%. Real or n=1826 noise? Worth investigating.
  - *Observations 5-6 (missing pair / timing / tone).* None.
- **Files:** sprint card + sweep report at `artifacts/sprint110-sweep-report.md` + per-size eval metrics at `artifacts/sprint110-{xs,sm,md,lg}-eval/metrics.json` + top-3 checkpoints per size + full JSONL traces per size.
- **Closed:** with an honest failure of the pre-registered pooled-linear gate and a strong regime-conditional signal. Sprint 111 opens next: either a diagnostic sprint (per-regime baselines + longer training probe at md) or context-length sweep at md.

### Sprint 108 (2026-08-24, closed) — AWS pre-flight tightening (review fixes)

- **Scope:** every finding in `reviews/aws-and-phase-g-review.md` §12. Five blockers, four should-fixes, one non-blocking `shutdown -h` correction, two runbook notes.
- **Dual contract:** signal (unchanged) + artifact (IAM role + instance profile + dedicated SG all live in AWS; managed policy at v3; `provision_gpu.sh` emits fully-populated RunInstances JSON; renamed env var propagated to train.py + tests + runbook; 623 tests pass).
- **Observation contract:** `pass_kind: architecture`, `determinism_budget: bit-deterministic`. Live dry-run resolved actual SG ID `sg-001faab872e311c38`, real instance profile name, real MetadataOptions. Test-side pins prevent regression on every review finding.
- **Rubber Duck Pass:**
  - *Observation 1 (Deny-general + Allow-scoped for pass-role).* Removing `iam:PassRole` from `DenyIAMEscalation` and adding a narrow `Allow` scoped to one role ARN + a `iam:PassedToService = ec2.amazonaws.com` condition is the canonical pass-role narrowing pattern. Deny beats Allow at the general layer; the narrow Allow lands cleanly.
  - *Observation 2 (SG resolution by name, not ID).* `provision_gpu.sh` resolves the SG by name at launch so the script does not carry a stale ID if the SG is ever recreated. Untrained checkpoint invariant under multiplier scaling — same monotone-loss single-trade condition Sprints 097/098 exposed.
- **Rubber Duck Pass:**
  - *Observation 1 (strict-cross semantics).* `crosses = (min < 0.0) AND (max > 0.0)`. Both strict. Flat-zero is not crossing; a positive-slope-but-touches-zero surface is not crossing. Named on card + module. The Layer-7 gate reads False on a flat-zero surface, correctly, because a flat-zero result is neither robust nor a crossing — it is silent.
  - *Observation 2 (linearization documented).* Sprint 096 established `slippage_frac × |size| ≈ kappa × size²`. Scaling `slippage_frac` by `m` scales the linearized kappa cost by `m`. The sweep tests robustness of Sharpe to that scaling.
  - *Observation 3 (nested-run pattern reused).* Third sprint using the pattern (096 trades → 098 capacity → 099 sensitivity). `dataclasses.replace(policy_template, ...)` overrides one knob per iteration; the rest of the walker stays untouched.
  - *Observations 4-6 (missing pair / timing / tone).* None.

On direction change AND current_position is not None: close at fill=`t+1`'s price → emit `POSITION_CLOSED` (close_kind="normal") + `TRADE_LEDGERED`.   - *Observation 1 (fill-price approximation named on card).* Fill at t+1's close price, not t+1's open. Documented in module docstring + sprint card + `close_kind` note. Symmetric across entries and exits so the round-trip cancels most of the bias.

Live smoke: 2015-2022 aligned parquet, 54,236 bars, CS finite mean 8.39 bps / median 5.69 bps / 5-95% range 0.44-24.65 bps / max 488.76 bps. 39% NaN pairs (Corwin-Schultz's documented negative-alpha boundary).

  - *Observation 1 (bias direction reversed from daily-bar literature).* Real SPY intraday BBO in 2015-2022 was ~0.5-1.5 bps; CS mean on 15-min bars is 8.39 bps — a 4-8× over-estimate. The Abdi-Ranaldo 2017 downward-bias finding applies to daily bars; sub-day bars flip direction because H-L captures within-bar noise not spread structure. The bias correction (Sprint 093) is a scalar multiply that handles either direction, so no code change is needed; the v1 report's cost-model section names the observed direction.
  - *Observation 2 (39% negative-alpha pairs).* Consistent with Corwin-Schultz Table III on liquid ETFs at short intervals. Not a defect. The remaining 33,103 finite pairs are ample for a mean estimate.
  - *Observations 3-6 (missing pair / order / timing / tone).* None.

Forward: embedder consumes `feats[key]` shape `[B, T=64, F_c]`, reshapes to `[B, 16, 4*F_c]`, per-channel projects to `[B, 16, d_model]`, sums; transformer stack processes 16 positions; output `[B, 16, V]`. Trainer: `_patch_targets(batch.targets, 4) = batch.targets[:, 3::4]` selects targets for input indices 4, 8, …, 64 corresponding to input bars start+4, start+8, ….
  - *Observation 1 (patching orthogonal to fusion).* Named on the card. When both `--patch-size 4` and `--fusion mixer` are specified, patching wins and mixer is ignored. Sweep scripts (Sprint 084+) skip the impossible combination.

Sprint 088+ candidate. Not blocking — invalid rows have `targets = -100` and PyTorch CE `ignore_index=-100` already excludes them from the loss.

### Sprint 025 (2026-08-11, closed)

- **Scope:** alignment pipeline (tech-arch §4.3 join_asof) + polars/pyarrow adoption.
- **Dual contract:** signal (4 align tags emit from live code: `ALIGNMENT_RUN_STARTED`, `ALIGNMENT_ROW_EMITTED`×520, `ALIGNMENT_RUN_COMPLETED`; `AS_OF_JOIN_MISS` verified in unit tests, not surfaced live because both channels had complete data) + artifact (105 tests pass; four tools green; live smoke writes 520-row parquet in 0.04s; wide format with per-channel close + known_at columns).
- **Observation contract:** `pass_kind: functional`, `determinism_budget: bit-deterministic`. Live smoke `uv run python scripts/align.py --month 2024-06`: exit 0, trace `logs/align-2024-06-0000000000000000/signals.jsonl` = 524 lines, parquet `data/aligned/align-2024-06-0000000000000000.parquet` = (520, 5).
- **Rubber Duck Pass:**
  - *Sequence narration.* SESSION_INIT with `run_kind=align` (first live emit at this run_kind), `vocab_version=0.3`, real git SHA. ALIGNMENT_RUN_STARTED with `run_id=align-2024-06-0000000000000000`, date range 2024-06-01 → 2024-06-30, channels `[market_context, target]` (deduped, sorted), target_symbol=SPY. `run_alignment` loads SPY + USO caches (~163KB + ~155KB), builds grid, calls `align_channels`. join_asof runs twice (once per channel), producing wide DataFrame. 520 ALIGNMENT_ROW_EMITTED fire (`missing_channels_count=0` for every row — both channels have full-month data). Zero AS_OF_JOIN_MISS. Parquet write. ALIGNMENT_RUN_COMPLETED with `total_rows=520`, `missing_fractions={target: 0.0, market_context: 0.0}`, `elapsed_seconds≈0.04`. SESSION_COMPLETE, `n_signals_emitted=524`.
  - *Observation 1 (test-writing surfaced buffer overflow, resolved-here).* First run of `test_run_alignment_writes_parquet_and_emits_summary` failed with `tags[0] == "ALIGNMENT_ROW_EMITTED"` because `StrictSignalEmitter(max_buffer=500)` dropped the ALIGNMENT_RUN_STARTED emit when ~1000 total emits overflowed the deque. Test helper bumped to `max_buffer=8192`. Production script uses default 500 because it verifies via the JSONL sink, not the buffer.
  - *Observation 2 (backward-join causality test, resolved-here).* First-pass test expected `grid=15:00 → close=541` because a bar `known_at=15:01` "was in that period." That's exactly the leakage `strategy="backward"` prevents. Corrected expectation: `grid=15:00 → close=540` (the bar known at 14:31), because the 15:01 bar is known AFTER the grid moment and therefore invisible. This is the alignment invariant working as designed.
  - *Observation 3 (Juneteenth grid rows, filed).* Naive weekday calendar keeps June 19 (Juneteenth, real US market closed) as a trading day. Grid holds 26 bars; join_asof backward-fills all 26 from June 18's last bar. Downstream feature computation would see stale prices for June 19. Filed under existing `pandas_market_calendars` drift-watchlist deferral.
- **Files:** `src/price_space_llm/alignment/__init__.py` (new), `src/price_space_llm/alignment/join.py` (new, ~230 lines), `scripts/align.py` (new, ~110 lines), `tests/test_alignment.py` (new, 11 tests), `pyproject.toml` (deps + version).
- **Closed:** clean.

### Sprint 024 (2026-08-11, closed)

- **Scope:** refactor `IngestionClient.call()` API (kwargs → callback); add `make_alphavantage_raw_fetcher` + `alphavantage_extract_metadata`; author `scripts/ingest.py`; live smoke.
- **Dual contract:** signal (5 ingest tags now emit from real code: `INGESTION_CALL_ISSUED`, `INGESTION_CALL_CACHED`, `RAW_OBSERVATION_WRITTEN` all fired from live traces; `SOURCE_FALLBACK_TRIGGERED` and `REVISION_LANDED` still test-only until a Polygon fetcher lands and a revision actually occurs) + artifact (94 tests pass; four tools green; live smoke produces 6-line first-run trace and 4-line second-run cache-hit trace; two ~160KB cache files under `data/raw/`).
- **Observation contract:** `pass_kind: functional`, `determinism_budget: statistically-deterministic`. Live smoke `uv run python scripts/ingest.py --month 2024-06 --fetcher alphavantage` exit 0. Trace payloads verified: `RAW_OBSERVATION_WRITTEN(channel=target, symbol=SPY, value_time=2024-06-29T01:00:00+00:00, rows_written=1235)` and same shape for USO with `rows_written=1203`.
- **Rubber Duck Pass:**
  - *Sequence narration.* First run: SESSION_INIT (`run_id=ingest-alphavantage-2024-06-0000000000000000`, config_hash sha256 of `month:manifest_hash`). Cache-miss path for SPY → `_primary_bucket.acquire()` (bucket fresh, 75 tokens) → INGESTION_CALL_ISSUED → live HTTP GET to `alphavantage.co/query?function=TIME_SERIES_INTRADAY&symbol=SPY&interval=15min&month=2024-06&outputsize=full&datatype=json&apikey=...` → response returns ~163KB JSON with 1235 bars → cache write to `data/raw/mcp_av/TIME_SERIES_INTRADAY/{sha256}.json` → `alphavantage_extract_metadata(response)` returns `{value_time: 2024-06-29T01:00:00+00:00, known_at: value_time+1min, rows_written: 1235}` → RAW_OBSERVATION_WRITTEN. Same sequence for USO. SESSION_COMPLETE at `n_signals_emitted=6`. Second run: cache-hit path for both channels; INGESTION_CALL_CACHED fires with `cache_key = sha256(tool, channel, symbol, params)`; no HTTP.
  - *Observation 1 (H9 finding, filed).* Alpha-Vantage's TIME_SERIES_INTRADAY defaults to `extended_hours=true` — SPY returned 1235 bars for June 2024, not the ~546 the probe's `EXPECTED_BARS_PER_TRADING_MONTH = 21 * 26` heuristic assumes. On over-count, `missing_fraction` clamps to 0 (harmless for probe verdicts). On under-count with an explicit `extended_hours=false` request, the same denominator would report ~0.08 missing on a genuinely complete regular-hours response and drop the channel wrongly. Filed for the sprint that first calls with `extended_hours=false`.
  - *Observation 2 (H9 finding, previously deferred).* `value_time = 2024-06-29T01:00:00+00:00 UTC` for the newest SPY bar. That's 21:00 EDT on 2024-06-28; the actual market close is 20:00 EDT. Off by one hour because `US_EASTERN_OFFSET = timedelta(hours=-5)` in `alphavantage.py` uses fixed EST and 2024-06 is DST. Documented at Sprint 019 close; still deferred; the extended-hours default means the newest bar isn't the market-close bar anyway (extended trading runs to 20:00 EDT for bars closing at 20:00 EDT which is 00:00 UTC), so the DST-wrong offset compounds with the extended-hours reality — the "newest bar" is legitimately post-market for regulars.
  - *Observation 3 (design confirmation, resolved-here).* The callback-based `.call()` API is honest — `extract_metadata` runs against the response after fetch, so the emitted RAW_OBSERVATION_WRITTEN carries real numbers from the vendor. First live run produced correct row counts (1235 SPY, 1203 USO) without any caller-supplied guesses. The Sprint 021 kwargs API would have required the CLI to pass `rows_written=???` before knowing the answer. Refactor was correct.
- **Files:** `src/price_space_llm/ingestion/client.py` (API refactor), `src/price_space_llm/ingestion/alphavantage.py` (raw fetcher + extractor), `scripts/ingest.py` (new CLI), `tests/test_ingestion_client.py` (11 tests updated for new API), `tests/test_alphavantage.py` (7 new tests for raw fetcher + extractor), `tests/test_ingest_cli.py` (5 new subprocess tests), `pyproject.toml` (version 0.11 → 0.12).
- **Closed:** clean.

### Sprint 023 (2026-08-11, closed)

- **Scope:** rip `_error_shim` + empty-series fill; wire `CHANNEL_FETCH_FAILED`.
- **Dual contract:** signal (five vocabulary tags now emit from honest inputs; `CHANNEL_FETCH_FAILED` fires on real Alpha-Vantage errors with the vendor's own error string) + artifact (80 tests pass; four tools green; live smoke produces 19-line trace with zero fill values).
- **Observation contract:** `pass_kind: functional`, `determinism_budget: statistically-deterministic`. Live smoke `uv run python scripts/probe_channels.py configs/channels/v1.json --fetcher alphavantage` returns exit 2 with two channels accepted and one dropped for `all_fetches_failed`.
- **Rubber Duck Pass:**
  - *Sequence narration.* SESSION_INIT at `run_id=probe-alphavantage-0000000000000000`, `vocab_version=0.3` (Sprint 022's default). Three channels probed: SPY yields 5 CHANNEL_PROBED with real UTC timestamps (`2015-06-01T09:00:00+00:00` earliest across the sample dates) and `missing_fraction` values in the expected range for Alpha-Vantage historical months; CHANNEL_COVERAGE_ASSESSED with `verdict=accepted`. VIX: 5 fetcher raises (`AlphaVantageResponseError: Invalid API call...`), each produces one CHANNEL_FETCH_FAILED, zero CHANNEL_PROBED, zero CHANNEL_COVERAGE_ASSESSED. USO parallels SPY. SESSION_COMPLETE at `n_signals_emitted=19, exit_code=0` (process_session internal); CLI wraps and returns 2 to the caller because VIX dropped.
  - *Observation 1 (design lesson, filed).* The failure mode I shipped in Sprint 020 was "the vocabulary refused, so I fabricated values until it stopped refusing." That is the exact anti-pattern SDD exists to prevent. The correct move at emit-time refusal is halt-and-articulate: stop, examine whether the vocabulary is genuinely insufficient, evolve if so, then wire. I did none of that. Two sprints later, at Architect prompt, I retracted the mis-framed Surfaced entries and executed the correct sequence. Diary entry names this as the load-bearing lesson from Sprints 022–023.
  - *Observation 2 (design choice, defensible).* `_extract_fetch_result` empty-series raises rather than returns `None`. Rationale: caller pattern is `result = fetcher(...); result["actual_frequency"]`; a `None` return would require every downstream field access to gate on `if result is None`. Raising forces the exception up to `probe_channel`'s try/except where the handling lives. Documented in the module docstring.
  - *Observation 3 (payload cap, resolved-here).* `error_message` capped at 1000 chars. Alpha-Vantage strings run ~150 chars; a hostile/misconfigured vendor could return a 100KB blob; unbounded trace payloads bloat JSONL and slow readers. 1000 keeps every real vendor error verbatim while capping the pathological.
- **Files:** `src/price_space_llm/ingestion/probe.py` (rewritten — try/except per date, `ChannelCoverage` timestamps `str | None`, verdict/reason on zero-observation case), `src/price_space_llm/ingestion/alphavantage.py` (empty-series raise), `scripts/probe_channels.py` (`_error_shim` deleted; unused imports removed), `tests/test_probe.py` (3 new tests), `tests/test_alphavantage.py` (empty-series test rewritten).
- **Closed:** clean.

No `REVISION_LANDED` because prior_hash was None. Test `test_fallback_fires_source_fallback_triggered`: primary raises RuntimeError → caught → fallback configured → emits `SOURCE_FALLBACK_TRIGGERED` with `from_source=mcp_av, to_source=polygon, reason=response_invalid` → `fallback_bucket.acquire()` → emits `INGESTION_CALL_ISSUED` with `source=polygon` → fallback returns dict → cache write to polygon path. The `symbol` was passed to `client.call()` as a separate kwarg but never entered the cache key. Cut on second pass; kept `released_at = known_at` (a reasonable simplification — the "when did the vendor release this data" clock is not currently distinguished from "when did we learn about it"; the alignment sprint refines).

- **Rubber Duck Pass:**
  - *Sequence narration.* Fresh alphavantage run: SESSION_INIT at t=0.0001 with `run_id=probe-alphavantage-0000000000000000`, `vocab_version=0.2`, real git SHA and config sha256. Then 15 CHANNEL_PROBED spanning SPY/VIX/USO × 5 sample dates. VIX's five probes each emit `missing_fraction=1.0` (shim path — Alpha-Vantage returned `Error Message: Invalid API call` for `^VIX` on TIME_SERIES_INTRADAY). SPY and USO probes carry real earliest/latest UTC timestamps and real `missing_fraction` values in the 0.0..0.9 range. CHANNEL_REJECTED × 1 fires for VIX (the aggregate verdict; per-date fetch errors show up in stderr, not in the trace). CHANNEL_COVERAGE_ASSESSED × 3 close each channel. SESSION_COMPLETE at t≈4.7s carries `exit_code=0, n_signals_emitted=21`. File line count matches.
  - *Observation 1 (vocabulary gap, surfaced-for-review).* Fetcher-error reason has no home in `CHANNEL_PROBED` payload — every field is strict-enum or ISO-datetime. Three separate emit-time crashes surfaced this: `actual_frequency="unknown"` rejected by the frequency enum, `earliest_timestamp=""` rejected by the datetime parser, `revision_behavior="error:AlphaVantageResponseError"` rejected by the revision-behavior enum. Each crash produced one more constraint on what the shim can fabricate. Final shape: `actual_frequency="15min"`, timestamps at epoch-zero, `revision_behavior="immutable"` — all vocabulary-legal, none accurate. The failure string lives on stderr only. Filed as a Surfaced-for-review vocabulary question (add `CHANNEL_FETCH_FAILED` tag vs extend `revision_behavior` enum).
  - *Observation 2 (payload anomaly, resolved-here).* `_extract_fetch_result` had the same `earliest_timestamp=""` bug on the empty-series path (200 OK with `{}` series map). Fixed to epoch-zero + `revision_behavior="empty_series"`. Test updated.
  - *Observation 3 (design change surfaced by use, resolved-here).* `run_id` was `probe-{seed:016d}` — mock and live runs collided on `logs/probe-0000000000000000/`. Added `{fetcher}` discriminator: `probe-{fetcher}-{seed:016d}`. Sprint 017's fixture updated to `probe-mock-0000000000000000` (the mock default now discriminates). No user-facing regression; the discriminator is a strict superset of the old shape.
  - *Observation 4 (timing surprise, surfaced-for-review).* JSONL sink opens with `"a"` (append). Three crashed runs during the smoke-loop left partial signals in the same trace file; `wc -l` showed 42 against `n_signals_emitted: 21`. Clean rerun after `rm -f` produced the expected 21 lines. Filed for a signals-hygiene sprint: sink should truncate on SESSION_INIT.
- **Files:** `scripts/probe_channels.py` (modified — flag, shim, run_id discriminator), `src/price_space_llm/ingestion/alphavantage.py` (modified — empty-series path), `tests/test_probe_cli.py` (modified — fixture path update + new test), `tests/test_alphavantage.py` (modified — empty-series assertion updated).
- **Closed:** clean.

The strict-extras posture caught one initial fixture where I passed `sample_date` as a `datetime.date` object where the vocabulary declares `date_iso` (string); fixed by calling `.isoformat()` at the emit site.

- **Rubber Duck Pass:** one iteration-count observation, resolved-here. First pass fed three values into the fake clock (`[100.0, 200.0, 200.0005]`), which the emitter exhausted on the fourth read (Signal reconstruction in the sink path calls monotonic separately from the parent's Signal). Fixed by padding the schedule with 32 copies of `200.0005` — every read after the reset returns the same value, so t stays 0.0005 regardless of how many monotonic reads the emit path makes.

### Sprint 004 (2026-08-10, closed)

- **Scope:** typed-payload enforcement (review §2) plus real-hex fixture rework (§5). Two files modified.
- **Dual contract:** signal (test-time only) + artifact (`uv run pytest tests/ -v` → 17 passed in 0.10s; `uv build` clean; smoke check on `run_kind='banana'` raises with the enum members named).
- **Rubber Duck Pass:**
  - *Sequence narration.* Ten new tests exercise enum, sha256 (length + hex class), int, bool-vs-int, uuid (via parser), datetime_utc, date_iso, list-element type, plus a canary that verifies valid payloads still pass. First run: 16 passed, 1 failed. Failure was `test_uuid_malformed_raises` — trade_id was declared `entity_ref<Trade>` in vocabulary round 4, not `uuid`. No field in v0.1 uses the `uuid` type through the vocabulary. Fix: test the `_parse_type("uuid")` checker directly rather than through emit. Second run: 17 passed.
  - *Observation 1 (vocabulary gap, resolved-here).* The `_check_uuid` code path is present but unreachable via the current vocabulary. Two options: remove `_check_uuid` (drop dead code) or keep and cover via parser tests (drop dead-code claim). Kept — a future vocabulary bump may add a genuine `uuid` field, and dropping the checker now means adding it back later. Parser-level test covers correctness.
  - *Observation 2 (payload anomaly, resolved-here).* Loose `_check_str` fallback in `_parse_type` for unknown type strings will silently pass anything that happens to be a string. If a future vocabulary declares a type the parser doesn't recognise, the check reads permissive instead of raising. Small risk; defensible as forward-compatibility. Documented in the code comment; not fixed.
- **Files:** `src/price_space_llm/signals.py` (modified — ~130 lines added for type checkers, parser, and `StrictSignalVocabulary.__init__`), `tests/test_signals.py` (modified — 10 new tests, fixtures use real hex).

### Sprint 001 (2026-08-10, closed)

- **Scope:** package scaffold + strict-validating signal emitter over the locked v0.1 vocabulary. Three code files + two ceremony inits.
- **Dual contract:** signal (test-time only; no pipeline emission) + artifact (all content assertions hold; all four command exit codes match).
- **Test outcome:** `uv run pytest tests/ -v` → 4 passed in 0.02s. Tests: `test_locked_vocabulary_loads_with_55_tags`, `test_unknown_tag_raises`, `test_missing_required_payload_raises`, `test_extra_payload_field_raises`.
- **Rubber Duck Pass:** structurally vacuous. The pass's six categories (missing pair / order violation / vocabulary gap / payload anomaly / timing surprise / tone trace) walk a signal trace. Sprint 001's four tests exercised raise paths only; no successful emits, no trace. Three code-side notes surfaced during test authoring, filed here as project-notes rather than as trace observations:
  - *Code note 1 (resolved-here).* Extra-field check runs after required-field check in `StrictSignalVocabulary.validate`. A payload with both errors surfaces missing-required first; the extra field never flags in the same pass. Required check is the more informative failure — no code change.
  - *Code note 2 (resolved-here).* `load_vocabulary` copies each tag's `note` field into the schema dict. Reference `SignalVocabulary` never reads it. Dead field kept — a diagnostic printer in a later sprint may want to name the tag by its note.
  - *Code note 3 (deferred).* `SignalEmitter._session_start` sets at module import, not at SESSION_INIT emit. `emit()`'s `t = time.monotonic() - self._session_start` reads relative to Python-process-start. Sprint 002's JSONL sink surfaces this as wrong `t` values. Fix: `StrictSignalEmitter.emit` resets `_session_start` when tag == 'SESSION_INIT'. Added to `## Drift watchlist`; Sprint 002 addresses.
- **Files:** pyproject.toml, src/price_space_llm/__init__.py, src/price_space_llm/signals.py, tests/__init__.py, tests/test_signals.py.
- **Artifacts installed side-effects:** `.venv/` (uv sync), `.pytest_cache/`. Both ignored per `.gitignore`.
- **Closed:** clean.
